## 2026-09-03b — Owner ruling: TUNNEL WALL CREST = DEM ALL THE WAY ROUND THE RAMP

* **THE WALL STAYS AT DEM (owner sim read of 1.0.275, OTHH).** "The wall should stay at DEM all the way around the tunnel ramp allowing the tunnel to descend to its full depth"; the mouth wall node stands the bore datum (`BRIDGE_ROAD_CLEARANCE_M` 5.1 m) above the ramp's mouth node; "no service road shape running around the outside of the tunnel wall". Spec `docs/specs/tunnel-wall-crest-dem-spec.md`; merged `7c0513cf` (lane/wallcrest). SUPERSEDES for wall crest bands: round-4 spec R5 / lead ruling 2026-08-10 (the transition law graded the crest DOWN to the ramp — the flat 4.00 m crest R5 called the defect IS the ruled surface), R16-2a's crest convergence, and `retaining_wall` in `groundside.TRANSITION_ROLES`. The §F1 station law and 2026-09-01c/e (foot retired, 0.6 m gap, one corridor-top value per station) STAND — the corridor-top value is the DEM.
* **MECHANISM (attributed, not reasoned):** the crest was derived twice — `bridges._CrestProfile` applied `transition_law_altitudes` toward the ramp at emit, and `finalize` → `apply_below_grade_transition` re-graded `retaining_wall` (and every plate outside the wall) toward the ramp again; the approach road's annulus remainder (a 1.8–5.1 m ribbon around the wall) shared the wall's outer-edge nodes and outranked it in `to_osm`. The 2026-08-30 "host WHOLE" clause had retired with the `tunnel_road` claim (08-31) and nothing replaced it.
* **LAW:** L1 crest = DEM by station (transition law DELETED from both emitters, 29f); L2 wall out of `TRANSITION_ROLES` — the wall is the discontinuity; L3 a ramp with a registered wall (`wall_band_owners`) is not a below-grade source for the R5 transition (unwalled shallow ramps and `tunnel_trench` unchanged); L4 service-road-family hosts are taken WHOLE alongside the run — partitioned at the ramp's far-end line, no width/area tolerance; `groundside_pavement` hosts keep 25e. Unmasked by L2: `finalize.deconflict_road_features` clipped wall pieces and kept misaligned altitudes (emit dropped them) — now NN-resamples.
* **MEASURED (OTHH closing build e936a0cb6ab2):** owner wall node −1.10 → 4.00 vs ramp −1.12 (5.12 m); ribbon −10051 gone; census law-true 1,620 → 1,528 (airside 1,202 = 1,202, groundside 282 → 190); acceptance wall_top_flat 5.1 → 3.45 (one 0.47 m² SW-fork crumb), ramp_wall_gap 23 → 23 (pre-existing SW fork-crumb class at 25.2540,51.6034 — owed), mouth_inventory 1 non-canonical → 1 (same SW fork site; wall R cover 0.15 → 0.97), actionable_sites 32 → 17. Twelve L4 host pieces removed (largest service_road #48 1,157 m² at the SW fork — owner eyeball owed).

## 2026-09-03d — Owner rulings: auto-patch-v2 (ground-up, side by side)

* **V2 IS THE PLAN OF RECORD FOR THE ENGINE'S FUTURE.** Build `auto_patch_v2` from the ground up beside v1 (`docs/specs/auto-patch-v2-plan.md`, appendices A/B): fully LAW-COMPLIANT patches (not byte-identical to v1 — v1's are not lawful), simpler, faster to generate and to apply to the mesh; every source file ≤ 1,000 lines (a larger file needs a written reason); format-agnostic GradedSurface for X-Plane Next-Gen (S2 tiles), the Ortho4XP `.osm` patch being one adapter. Triggered by the week review (`review-20260903-week.md`): auto_patch 225,964 lines vs upstream Ortho4XP 17,979.
* **Solver:** add OSQP to the engine freeze if it measurably beats scipy/HiGHS on the v2 QP (measure, then add).
* **Law tables:** whichever is simpler; no value in retargeting v1's census to a new law source — v1 keeps `check_grade` until retirement; v2's `law/` is v2's own single source, cross-validated against v1's census as the oracle.
* **Loaders and classifiers are REWRITTEN** for v2 with v1 as the reference, cleaner and faster; nothing is carried by inheritance.
* **Mesh-apply bar:** tile mesh wall time and constrained-edge count with the v2 patch vs v1's on the same tile — v2 no slower, fewer edges.
* **Models:** Fable for ALL design, specs and reviews; implementation on whatever is most efficient (Opus by default once `.claude/hooks/agent_guard.py` admits it).
* **2026-09-03e (owner):** the v2 LAW TABLE is a HUMAN-READABLE, EDITABLE DATA FILE — adjusting a cap or adding a family must never require a code change. Implementation: TOML files under `src/auto_patch_v2/law/` (stdlib `tomllib`; comments carry meaning, unit and ruling id per key), a typed schema + loader that validates and fails loudly, and a twin that cross-checks the values against v1's constants. No numeric law value may live in Python.

## 2026-09-03h — Owner rulings: pads yield; Fable for implementation

* **APRON RELIEF: PADS YIELD.** Over real relief a building pad YIELDS so that it stays connected to its apron and the apron stays within its own grade law. The apron membrane is senior to the pad seat: the pad is a rigid flat group whose level is set by its apron contact (weld = value, 09-01g), never a pin the apron must climb to. Closes the plan's R4 charter question and the R1.4 residual class (1,058 HECA pass-2 apron↔apron rows across ~10 m relief; 2.84 m / 3.4 m steps at the R1.3 sites). v2: encoded in `law/structures.toml [building_pad]` and the pad constraint generator; v1: a pad-seat mechanism round measured on HECA.
* **MODEL: Fable approved for implementation lanes too** (higher success rate); the agent guard stays as it is.
* **2026-09-03i (owner, generalises 03h):** SENIORITY FOLLOWS FROM BEING GOVERNED. Anything NOT covered by a grade law yields to everything that is — pads yield because no grade law governs a pad, not because a pad-specific rule says so. The precedence table therefore has two tiers by construction: governed surfaces (a cap in the law tables) in their stated order, then everything ungoverned (pads, ungraded ground, walls, strips beyond the zones) which takes its level from what it touches. v2: `law/precedence.toml` states the principle and the loader derives the ungoverned tier from "no cap in rulesets"; a new surface class without a cap is junior by omission, no code change. v1: the R4 pad mechanism is one instance.

## 2026-09-04i — Owner rulings answering 03k / 04f (v2 law)

* **03k → PRIORITY IS THE LAW'S, TERRAIN NEVER BLOCKS.** The DEM is not always reliable. A feasible solution exists for every airport. ALL pavement must comply with the law, and pavement always overrides terrain when needed. When two governed surfaces contradict across relief, the LAW's priority order (`precedence.toml`) decides which yields — the junior governed surface yields to the senior (taxiways yield to runways, aprons to taxiways, …), and the DEM preference yields to every governed surface. No terrace minting, no "accept as geometry". v2: DEM rows are preferences only, never constraints that can make the set infeasible; the solver's tiers follow the precedence order. Unblocks M5 (HECA/KCLT).
* **04f-1 → RESTORE AND RE-BAKE EVERY BUILD.** Every build that generates a new patch restores the scenery pack's objects to their authored state before reading, and re-bakes them against the newly created terrain — otherwise the objects would not comply with the terrain the patch produced. v2 reads the RESTORED pack, never the previously re-baked state on disk; the re-bake is a product of every v2 build (the v1 `.anchor_bak` restore + post-mesh rebake machinery is the reference).
* **04f-2/3 → BELOW-GRADE OBJECTS GET THEIR CUTOUTS.** The terrain cutouts for below-grade objects like OTHH's drainage must be created correctly for every airport; an area floor or covered-fraction constant that refuses a real drainage bowl is a defect, not a law. Admission is by the object's own geometry (a closed below-grade region with a genuine solid floor), and every refusal names its reason.
* **04g-1 / 04g-2 / 04d → SIM CHECK**, with locations supplied by the session (below in STATUS).

## 2026-09-04o — Owner ruling: feasibility propagates along TAXI ROUTES from the runway thresholds, never along straight chords

* "Feasible elevations propagate out from runway thresholds and have to follow taxi routes … an apron very close laterally to the centre of a runway could have a big elevation difference because aircraft can't travel straight across the grass; they have to follow taxi routes, which is what follows the grade cap." The distance that prices a grade between two airside vertices is the ROUTE distance through pavement along the taxi network (v1's route-metric reach band, `building_feasibility.reach_band_unified`; memory `reach-follows-centerlines`), never the plan chord. This CLARIFIES 2026-08-27 (airside no-step): the no-step law forbids STEPS — pairs adjacent along pavement — and its 150 m / K=16 direct-distance window must not bind pairs whose only connection is across grass. v2: `constraints/no_step.py` pairs are formed along pavement (route distance through the planar map's pavement faces / taxi network, or adjacency only), and the census family reads the same population; v1's constants (window 150 m, K 16) are re-derived as route distances or retired. Measured trigger: CYXY apron shape 156 held 3.4–4.7 m under the lidar terrain by direct chords to runway vertices while its route to the runway is ~250 m at 1.5 %.
* Also ruled: a service road that can lawfully follow the terrain (CYXY shape 153, 2.6 % on 1 m lidar) is a question of intent — the owner will say whether roads seek level; until then roads follow terrain within their cap.

## 2026-09-04t — Owner rulings answering 04s (four)

* **(1) LAST RESORT = LEAST TOTAL VARIANCE FROM LAW.** Where the hard set is infeasible at a site (HECA's hangar row), the solver may relax ALL THREE populations together — a slightly over-cap apron, a slightly sloping pad contact, a slightly over-cap slope between buildings (never a cliff/terrace) — choosing the combination that minimises the TOTAL variance from law (a quadratic spread across the IIS rows, not L1's concentration on one row), and only for the rows an IIS names. No terrace minting.
* **(2) CAP BY EDGE PORTION.** A junction or road MOUTH joining or leaving an apron keeps the junction's / road's own cap; a junction or road sharing a LONG EDGE with an apron takes the stricter (apron) cap only on the portion ALONG the apron; portions that leave the apron return to their normal cap. Refines owner 2026-07-06 (v1 `_body_cap_unbounded` applied the apron cap to the whole junction body) — the oracle reader follows the same portion rule.
* **(3) TAXIWAY CAP CHANGES AT CENTRELINE INTERSECTIONS.** A taxiway's letter (cap) applies from its intersection with another taxiway onward, per stretch — at CYXY, taxiway G's 3 % begins at its intersection with E (60.7079446, −135.0697651): the next G node (60.7078279, −135.0704243) may already differ by 3 %. Junction faces carry the cap of the stretch they belong to; strictest-of-chains (04q-2) is superseded.
* **(4) ROADS: THE CORE SMOOTHS FIRST.** Ortho4XP's own road smoothing (`include_roads` / `clamp_road_network`, RULINGS 2026-08-31 "leverage the core") is applied to road profiles FIRST; v2 adds smoothing only where a road is still over its cap afterwards. A road following a lawful 2.6 % terrain slope (CYXY 153) is therefore the core's smoothed profile, not v2's DEM preference.

## 2026-09-04u — Owner sim read of v2 at CYXY, round 3 (app 1.0.281: "looking really good")

* **Weld gap** at 60.7081013,−135.0771456: cross_connector 101 (pav18) and 208 (pav5) share vertex −2366 at 703.12, but junction 211 (pav5) runs 0.47 m from it with no shared edge — a grass sliver narrower than the identity spacing (0.5 m) survived the planar overlay and the mesh drapes it as a cliff. LAW: two pavement faces whose boundaries lie within the identity spacing of each other WELD (shared edge/vertices) at planar build; no sliver of unowned ground may exist between pavements. Same class as HECA's near-coincident rim (04s).
* **Shape 106** (dsf:pol17 + pol20 + pol123) is NOT apron: groundside parking lots served only by the service road that connects from the apron at 60.7122971,−135.0745001 up to the lot at 60.7125394,−135.0752877 (1206 route 50 / routes 4–6). Classifier defects: an "open" page with no taxi centreline, no startups and no apron evidence defaulted to APRON (the default is groundside: lot if a road/route reaches it, else groundside pavement); a 1206 route reaching a page counted as 0 m of road. RULED: open pavement is never apron by default — apron needs evidence (stands, taxi centreline, apron name/aeroway, or an apron-named page); a route/road reaching a page is road evidence for it.
* **Parking lot 87**, node at 60.714258,−135.0766894 is WELDED to a building pad and pulled down with it. RULED: groundside pavement (lots, roads) never welds to a building pad — it keeps the 0.6 m setback (09-01i cut-back) on every pad it touches, airside-touching or not, so a pad's level cannot pull groundside down; the pad welds to airside pavement only (03h).

## 2026-09-04x — relaxation merged (04t-1); three spawner rulings

## 2026-09-05aa — Owner verdict on the HECA limiting-chain KML: the route graph follows EVERY curve of the taxi routes — never a chord across open pavement, never an edge

* Owner read of `HECA_limiting_chain.kml` (v2 route graph at lane/v2relaxfull 11a19b99): the green leg (pav91 → pav101 as the graph saw it) is all RUNWAY EDGE; the yellow taxi-only leg is right until 30°06'59.81"N 31°24'50.24"E and then cuts diagonally away from the centreline, jogs, and crosses open grass straight to the runway (the 2,382 m pav130 chord and the 1,467 m runway chord); the red band leg cuts straight across as well (apron perimeters at 1 %). RULED: the route graph for reach bands and no_step distances is the 1202 CENTRELINE NETWORK ONLY — every taxi centreline stretch at its stretch cap following every curve and turn, the runway centrelines at the runway cap, the crossings — with NO ring edges, NO stretch/apron/junction chords, NO apron perimeters, NO pad frontage hops. A pavement vertex off a centreline attaches to ITS OWN face's centreline at the nearest station by ONE lateral hop over the perpendicular distance at the face's transverse cap (a runway edge vertex to the runway centreline at `transverse_max`; a taxiway edge to its stretch at the taxi transverse cap; an apron vertex to the nearest taxilane crossing or touching it at the apron cap; a pad through its apron frontage). A vertex with no centreline to attach to has no reach band. This is v1's model ("reach follows centerlines"); everything else since 05v was an approximation of it. Spec §8; lane `v2relaxfull5`.

## 2026-09-05ab — lane v2relaxfull5: the graph is centreline-true; the taxi WITHIN-SHAPE chord is the last shortcut (spawner ruling)

RULED (spawner, 05aa applied to the shape law): a taxi-family within-shape row prices its pair over the CENTRELINE ROUTE distance between the two vertices' attachment stations (hop + centreline path + hop through the route graph), never the chord; a pair with no route between them gets no within-shape row (the transverse law, the junction mesh and no_step govern adjacency). Apron chords at 1 % stay (the apron law is all directions; relaxable). Also: a PAD's attachment to the graph is its CONTACT — the apron vertex it is welded to or its frontage row's apron vertex (0 m) — never a route hop; the lane deleted it with the frontage kind and CYXY minted 2 building rows (0.25 m over 0.5 m at building6/7): restore it. Accepted deviations: the hop prices transverse × perpendicular + centreline cap × the walk to the foot; hops are leaves (never transited); crossing entries are nodes; the planar breakline tolerance fix (`max(0.6·grid, weld spacing)`, HECA taxi136 chain restored). Spec §9; lane `v2relaxfull6`.

## 2026-09-06n — Owner KML verdict on the sag chain: "not a valid route — only taxiway centreline routes count; no apron except a route through it; the red hop crosses two separate aprons with a lawful step" — RULED

* The owner's gold route (their KML over ours): 23R threshold → the parallel taxiway → the 05C/23C hold, **3,231.34 m** — at 1.5 % = 48.47 m, ceiling 109.13 m at the hold, line 115.2 → the route-only law sets a **6.1 m bow** at that station (v2 holds 107.43, −7.8; v1 −9.3). Our binding chain (39 rows) ran 1,937 m of it through apron pav132's edge-portion and frontage-chord rows and crossed from apron cell #364 to #363 where no traffic passes. RULED (owner's law, spawner's mechanism): (1) FEASIBILITY IS THE ROUTE'S — a runway's or taxiway's elevation may be chained only through taxi-centreline rows (the chain law 05ac) and the runway's own rows; apron rows (within-shape chords, edge portions, frontage, pad welds) never carry reach between two taxi routes; (2) two apron cells that no taxi route joins are SEPARATE TERRACES — their shared boundary is a lawful step: a terrace joint (the groundside `terrace_joints` machinery: split vertices at the joint, no row across it, the joint declared in the sidecar so the census reads it as lawful); (3) within one apron cell the apron law (1 % all directions, 05ae chords inside the face) stands; (4) a taxi route THROUGH an apron couples only the apron vertices along that route (the taxilane's own stretch rows and lateral hops), never the whole cell to another route. ## 2026-09-07c — OWNER: an apron point's route is a VISIBLE PATH inside its apron shape to a taxi route, then centrelines only — no shortcuts across aprons (answers 07a-1 and 06r-1; extends 06n INSIDE a cell)

The lines don't even reach either runway, and the red line is crossing between two separate aprons that are not connected, AND wherever there is a taxi route, we always have to follow that, no shortcuts across aprons. For any given point on an apron, its route to runways must be a visible path (cannot exit the apron shape) to a taxi route, then along taxi centerlines only."
* The impact, stated once: the chain (`HECA_v2routecap3_binding_chain.kml`, 39 hops) runs runway hold → 23C stub → parallel taxiway → cross connector → junctions → #364's NORTH contact (hops 1–15, all taxi/junction rows), then the red chord across the cell (16–17), then junctions → the 23R end (18–39). The taxi route between the two contacts is 2,725 m; the chord 1,117 m; the runway is dragged by the difference (07a ablation: runway + routes alone puts the hold at 109.3 m = the owner's 109 m).
* RULED (owner's law; spawner's mechanism, spec `apron-reach-territory-spec.md`): (1) REACH: every apron vertex's budget from the runways is the least over its CONTACTS (taxi-centreline stations on the cell boundary or on a route through the cell) of [the apron cap × the in-shape VISIBLE path length to the contact] + [the route graph's budget from the contact] — the apron shape is never exited, no chord across the cell, no ring edge, no road, no junction body is a route. (2) TERRITORIES: the cell is partitioned by nearest contact (in-shape path); two adjacent territories whose contacts' route ceilings differ by more than the apron cap can hold along the in-shape path between them are SEPARATE TERRACES — the owner's "two separate aprons that are not connected" — and their boundary is a JOINT (06n machinery: split vertices, no row across, declared in the sidecar, keep-outs unchanged). Territories no route reaches are islands (06n). (3) The joint line CONTINUES through every face that spans the same two territories — junction bodies, roads, pads (a road ramps to the joint at its own cap on each side; a pad belongs to one territory) — so no surface family of any role bridges what the routes do not (07a: apron chords, junction mesh, taxi-ring box chains and road profiles were each a shortcut). (4) 06w's tiers stand INSIDE a territory (prefer 1 %, max 1.5 %); the back-edge class is owed. 07a-1 is answered (a): terrace, never steeper, never accept.
* Bars at HECA (the 06n bar restored): the 05C/23C hold at the 109 m intersection ≤ 6.1 m under the line (+ K), the ridge minimising curvature, the `why` chain taxi-centreline / crossing / runway rows ONLY, joints declared and read lawful (census ≤ 14, no DEFECT), six halves ≤ 1.535 %, CYXY/OTHH/LEMD verify 0 with their joints quoted. Lane `v2terrace2` (Fable).

## 2026-09-07d — OWNER: back edges need no fans or zones — the pair between two pad corners across a gap simply carries 5 %

* Owner: "we are creating a patch file that specifies the elevations we want at specific nodes, then the engine applies that to the mesh, automatically interpolating with the base DEM. If we have a back apron node at one pad corner at one elevation, and then a 10 m gap and another pad that we want to be lower we just set the elevation for that node so it would be a 5 % grade."
* RULED (owner's law; spawner's mechanism): 06w (3)'s `plan_fan_ramp_zones` port is WITHDRAWN. The back-edge class is a PAIR CAP: an apron ring edge (or an apron pair under the identity gate) whose two vertices are frontage/contact vertices of two DIFFERENT pads, on a ring run that no taxi route or stretch touches (the back edge between buildings), carries `apron_fan_ramp_max` (5 %, `rulesets.toml`, the 08-24 value) as its HARD cap in place of `max` (1.5 %); the 1 % preference row stays (06w). Nothing else changes: the mesh interpolates the DEM between the two nodes. Oracle lockstep: the census reads the same pairs at 5 % (the `back_edge` reading beside `apron_over_preference`); v2 verify likewise. Twin: two pads 10 m apart on an apron's back edge with a 0.5 m step between their corners → feasible, read lawful; 0.6 m → a row. Implementer: a short lane after `v2terrace2` (same file `constraints/apron.py`), or folded into that lane's close if it finishes with budget.

* Owner: the pack's apron polygon joins the two aprons there; "maybe we can tell based on the taxi routes that serve the two aprons from opposite ends but don't connect through here". Measured: the point lies inside #364 (pav132, 176,487 m²); the polygon is 126 m wide at that point (a wide connection, not a sliver weld) — so 06n's weld rule cannot see it and 07c's territory rule is the instrument: the two halves' contacts are reached from opposite ends and no route crosses the connection, so the territory boundary — the joint — falls across it. ACCEPTANCE SITE for lane `v2terrace2`: the minted joint polyline crosses within 60 m of 30.127729, 31.412022 (checked at merge; the owner reads the joint KML).

## 2026-09-07f — lane v2terrace2 CLOSED unmerged (f25ffcf5): the in-cell face cut is refuted on geometry; the spec's reach metric was the shortcut; construction moves to a PRE-ARRANGEMENT POLYGON CUT

* Lane findings (kept): pav132 is ONE apt.dat 110 polygon (509,514 m², 6 holes) that classification cuts into 28 faces — no weld to exploit; §2's literal metric (`cap·d_inshape + budget`) mints NO joint (the south contact "owns" the north contact: 81.67 + 0.015 × 541 = 89.8 < 125.16 — the apron itself became the route); agreement is not transitive (pairwise only); the partition must run over the welded pavement complex, not per face; cutting existing faces along a geodesic seam through ~6 junction faces fails: refused cuts, end copies inside the identity spacing on 0.5 m arcs, and DEAD-END lane stations (taxi202/205/206, no route to a runway) re-join the halves under 06n's "station on the boundary" rule. HECA: bow −10.40, DEMOTED, census 112 FAIL, verify 43, solve 458 s. CYXY minted 5 small joints 06n had not. Not merged; branch is the record. Twins and the census table stand as reference.
* RULED (spawner, spec §2a): (1) TERRITORY = nearest RUNWAY-CONNECTED contact (a station on a route the route graph joins to a runway threshold; a dead-end lane's stations serve no territory and never join cells); the metric decides only the PREDICATE (pairwise, adjacent territories). (2) The joint is a CUT OF THE RAW PAVEMENT POLYGON before the planar arrangement: the shortest chord of the polygon (holes respected) that separates the two territories' contact sets and crosses no runway-connected centreline — the "neck" (owner 07e: the pack's incorrect connection; at HECA the 126 m chord near 30.127729, 31.412022 is the expected answer). The two pieces are then ordinary cells and 06n's between-cell machinery (join only through runway-connected stations; split copies; declaration) does the rest — no in-face seam, no interior joint vertices. (3) Faces of other roles (junction bodies, roads, pads) inside the same pavement polygon are cut by the same chord at classification (they are pieces of the same 110 polygon or overlap it), so the seam is one polyline through the complex; a road crossing it ramps on each side at its own cap. (4) A pavement whose contacts all agree (the predicate false for every adjacent pair) is never cut. Lane `v2terrace3`.

## 2026-09-07g — OWNER reframes the route law: an apron is a route link only when there is no other way between runways; every other node takes the shortest visible in-shape path to a centreline route, then the route (supersedes 07f's polygon cut)

* Owner: "the only time an apron can be counted as part of a centerline route is when there is no other way for the route to connect between runways. Nodes not on the route must take the shortest visible path to a centerline route without leaving the apron, then follow the main route."
* RULED (owner's law; spawner's mechanics — no geometry): (1) THE ROUTE NETWORK is the 1202 centrelines joined to the runways (05aa/06n unchanged); an apron pavement joins the network as a link ONLY when a runway (or a route system) is otherwise unreachable — then the shortest in-shape path across it, at the apron cap, is the link (the feasibility fallback; report every airport where it fires). (2) SERVING CONTACT: every pavement node not on a route is served by the runway-connected route contact it reaches by the shortest visible in-shape path (never leaving its pavement complex); its reach band = the contact's band ± the apron cap × that path (`routes.reach` extended by the in-shape leg). (3) JOINTS WITHOUT CUTS: two adjacent nodes served by contacts whose bands disagree by more than the surface can hold between them (07f's pairwise predicate) form a JOINT EDGE — no row of any family is priced across it (within-shape chords, edge portions, junction mesh, box pairs, hops, road profiles, no_step), it is declared in the sidecar `terrace_joints` so both instruments read the step as lawful, and the mesh builds the step from the two node elevations (owner 07d: "we just set the elevation for that node"). No polygon cut, no split copies, no seam geometry: the joint is the label boundary. (4) A road crossing a joint: its profile rows stop at the joint; report the step the road takes (a ramp needs a ruling). (5) Everything else stands: 06n between cells (a cell no runway-connected station touches is an island), 06t, 06w, 07d.
* At HECA the label boundary of pav132's complex falls where the north and south contact sets meet — the owner's 07e neck (30.127729, 31.412022): the acceptance site. Lane `v2terrace3` is redirected to this construction (its polygon-cut work discarded; its partition/predicate kept).

Twins 12 + 1; suite 711 passed.
* HECA (`HECA_20260907T124610`): hard set FEASIBLE, no relaxation (was 184 rows), solve **47 s** (was 223), wall 193 s; hold v1444 **108.73** (was 107.89; owner 109.1); bow **−9.16** (was −10.38); census adjudicated **8** (terrace_joint_route 2, strip_seam_tear 5, vertex_to_edge_step 1); v2-verify 95 (within_shape 63 stub|stub at 1.51–1.55 % — owed, lateral_contiguity 26, strip_seam_tear 5, step 1); 73 joints / 19 label boundaries, max step 9.45 m; the boundary 47.8 m from the owner's 07e site; the `why` chain from the hold is pure centreline law (66 hops, Σ 48.08 vs the owner's 48.47 — 26 m of route along junction #389's ring, the last 0.4 m). CYXY 7.3 s verify 0 (joints 3), OTHH verify 0 (joints 15, steps 0.00), LEMD verify 20 (control on main being built). Fallback links 0 everywhere. Deviations accepted: the in-shape reach band is not a solver row (non-transitive agreement made it infeasible; the kept rows carry the reach); road corridors are labelled.
* THE RESIDUAL BOW ATTRIBUTED (spawner, `why` on the ridge minimum v1480 = 105.83 at station 2,696): hops 1–36 are the runway's OWN `runway_vertical_curve` rows at their bound from the hold (108.73, s ≈ 2,266) to the minimum — the route envelope descends INTO the hold at 1.5 % along the parallel taxiway (each metre of runway is a metre of route to 23R), and ICAO's K (`vertical_curve_k_m`, radius 30,000 m) needs ≈ 450 m to turn a −1.5 % grade around, losing ½·g²·K ≈ 3.4 m past the hold (measured 2.9). Not a shortcut: the law as the owner ruled it (routes at 1.5 % to the hold; minimum curvature; K hard) puts the runway's low point ≈ 450 m beyond the 109 m intersection, ≈ 3 m below it.
* OWNER QUESTION 07h-1: the hold is at 108.7 ≈ 109; the runway's minimum is 3 m lower and 450 m along, by the K law. (a) Accept — the ICAO curve is the law; (b) soften K at HECA (ICAO 3.1.16 is a recommendation; K = 10,000 m would lose ≈ 1.1 m); (c) something else about the approach side (the envelope descends at 1.5 % because the parallel taxiway route shortens metre for metre — nothing in the route law changes that). Recommendation: (a).
* Merge after the LEMD control: the branch is a strict improvement everywhere measured (bar ≤ 6.1 m not met — residual 3.06 m, owner sign-off requested with 07h-1).

## 2026-09-08d — HECA v1 vs v2 on ONE tree and frame (scout `hecav1v2`): v1 FILLS and CUTS to the threshold chord and lets every surface yield; v2 fits the DEM and holds every surface law hard — the sim prefers v1 (owner question 08d-1); OTHH's 95 object writes attributed

* Instruments: v1 control `HECA_20260908T073420` (813 s, 3,722 shapes, v1's usual uncertified exit) vs v2 `HECA_20260907T124610`, both censused law-true, DEM through v2's production loader. Census: **v2 8 adjudicated airside; v1 1,065 airside + 1,741 groundside** (airside_no_step 461, within_shape 377, transverse 645, road cross-sections 357; v1 junction max edge grade 9.1 %). The owner's sim verdict ("v2 much worse almost everywhere") is the REVERSE of the instruments: v1 breaks the letter of the law 1,065 times and looks right.
* WHAT DIFFERS (z_v2 − z_v1 over 21,440 v2 nodes: mean −0.43, p10 −4.08, p90 +2.49; 57 % differ by > 1 m, 917 by > 6 m):
  1. RUNWAYS. v1 anchors each profile to the CIFP threshold chord and FILLS: 05R/23L rides +4.70 m of fill (bow −4.25); v2 fits the DEM (20/m) and sags to −9.61 at the DEM. 05C/23C bows match (−9.17 v2 / −9.53 v1) but v2 holds the DEM for 1.6 km then dives at 1.38 %, v1 descends steadily from 05C. 05L/23R: v2 lifts the middle +7.5 m above the DEM (a hump) — interventional: dropping the taxi surface families (`taxi_centreline, taxi_chain, taxi_box, junction_mesh, no_step_pairs`) takes face 35's z−DEM from +1.98/+7.61 to +0.09/+1.59: the hump is the taxi family's hard 1.5 % surface law in its redundant forms pulling the runway up to the NW complex (07a's finding again, the other way).
  2. TAXIWAYS/STRIPS. v1 fills 4–6 m across the SW strips and connectors (sites 30.105,31.398 etc. — v1 +5.06 above DEM, v2 +0.50); v2 sits on the DEM. v1's strips ride the fill (+0.42 mean); v2's −0.36.
  3. APRONS OVER RELIEF. pav132: v1 grades ONE continuous cut 7–10 m below the DEM, 0 joints (v1's `APRON_TERRACE_MAX_STEP_M = 2.0` is LAW: a joint may carry ≤ 2 m, else the apron grades through); v2 hugs the DEM and declares 73 joints, 8 of them 5.9–9.45 m (`emit.toml max_step_m = 2.0` is report-only in v2). Every v2 joint over 2 m sits where v1 had no step at all (≤ 0.68 m).
  4. THE OWNER'S EXCEPTION SITE 30.1139552, 31.4095465 (pav131, 20 m of relief across the cell): v1 has a ~3 m apron-to-groundside step within 15 m of the point (the one thing the owner disliked — `apply_lateral_contiguity_law` + groundside at DEM); v2 makes it a 6.20 m declared joint (27) between face 215 and a 3-node SLIVER face 269 of the SAME pavement — twice as deep, and lawful on paper.
* OTHH (the owner: "should not require any object modifications"): the owner's 07:20 tile build wrote **95 objects** (84,163 vertices). Attributed: Bridges Bus 45 — the anchors sample the tile mesh at 0.00 m on WATER (the canal) and `rebake.py:313-318` founds the seat on that sample (no water check; `water_founds_seat=false` applies to parts only) → every delta is against a base 3.96 m too low; Terminal 20 — the elevated `TerminalRoads` decks' rings sample flat 3.96, so the deck TOP is seated at ground: −10.87 m (`rebake.py:239-249`, exempt from the threshold by `structure_seat_threshold_exempt`); Emiri 4 — parts read the raw inset DEM outside the patch; tunnels middle-west/east — plate stations sample the trench drop (−2.30/−2.09; 08a's rim inset will make this WORSE); Dewatering 18 — cut compensations (needed) plus one +0.001 m no-op write. 77 of 95 are seat-law artefacts. Rules: an anchor on water or on a flat-candidate site takes Z0 as ground (or the unit is HELD); a deck seat requires abutment relief at its ring; plate stations sample outside the rim; the threshold exemption never passes a write under `min_delta_m`; the flat datum extends under object footprints. Lane `v2othhseat`.
* THE INTENT QUESTION 08d-1 (owner): v1's HECA is the PRIORITY model — runway on the threshold chord (fill/cut), taxiways then aprons CONFORM by yielding (grades over cap where the runway demands), no steps over 2 m; v2's is the HARD-LAW model — every surface at its cap, the runway on the DEM, relief absorbed by declared steps. The owner's own rulings since 09-04 built the hard model ("zero airside violations", 06w max 1.5 %, 06n/07g joints) and the owner's sim read prefers v1's. RECOMMENDATION (spawner): adopt v1's priority model in v2's machinery — (1) the runway objective fits the THRESHOLD CHORD, senior to the DEM fit (K and max grade stay hard); (2) the surface families beyond the runway-connected route graph (junction mesh, short-pair box, no_step pairs, apron chords/edge portions, road profiles) become PREFERENCES with escalation ceilings, junior to the chord term — they yield where the runway demands, exactly v1; (3) a declared joint carries ≤ 2 m HARD; a boundary that cannot close within 2 m is not a joint, the cell grades through (v1); (4) at the owner's pav131 site the apron edge RAMPS to the groundside at the groundside cap (5 %) instead of stepping. The census will then read violations at HECA as v1's does; the sim is the acceptance (pre-ship mode). Spec `heca-v1-parity-spec.md`; lane `v2chord`. The owner may veto before the merge; the lane's closing build quotes both instruments.

## 2026-09-08k — OWNER: joints exist ONLY at physical separations — touching or overlapping pavement is ONE shape; > 0.5 m apart is a separate apron/lot that may step; no other step in an apron; single solve pass (supersedes 06n's station rule, 07g's label boundaries and the 2 m joint passes)

Ideally we would be able to do this with a single solve pass."
* RULED (owner's law; spawner's mechanism): (1) SHAPES: the connected components of touching/overlapping pavement (the 04u weld at the identity spacing already unions ≤ 1 m gaps — the owner's 0.5 m is the law: `terrace.separation_m = 0.5`), EXCEPT that a connection through a NARROW MOUTH or a SERVICE ROAD does not join two bodies (default `narrow_mouth_max_m = 12` — wider than any service road, narrower than a code-C taxiway — pending the owner's number). (2) JOINTS: only the boundaries between different shapes (a gap > 0.5 m, a narrow mouth, a road between) may step, declared as today; NO joint inside a shape — 06n's "joined by a station" partition and 07g's label boundaries are WITHDRAWN, the 2 m law and its re-solve passes DELETED. (3) INSIDE a shape the apron surface is whatever its connecting taxiways demand: the apron rows are preferences (1 % preferred, 1.5 % max, yielding beyond as the 08d model) with NO hard ceiling inside a shape — a step is never lawful there, a steep grade is. (4) ONE solve pass: joints are geometric (known before the solve), nothing is re-solved on a built step. Consequence at HECA: pav132's 126 m neck is inside one shape → no wall, the north and south halves grade through (34 m over 1.1 km ≈ 3.1 %); the hangar island #363 (1,193 m against junction #40, no route) stays a separate shape only if a gap > 0.5 m or a road separates it — else it is part of the shape and grades with it.

## 2026-09-08t — OWNER INTERVIEW on 1.0.295: v2 "still follows the DEM too closely — unrealistic undulation; v1 grades, fills and cuts to smoothed pavement areas; something got lost in v2's design and should be simpler and faster" — THE DESIGN SURFACE (supersedes the DEM-fit objective and the hard-law acceptance)

* Owner's purpose: simulated airports for pilots — terrain as realistic as possible. Eight answers, all ruled:
  1. OBJECTIVE: each pavement body is the SMOOTHEST surface — MINIMUM CURVATURE in every direction (a stiff sheet pinned at its contacts); the DEM is only a DATUM (thresholds and the surrounding ground), never a per-vertex pull on pavement. Runways: the threshold chord + vertical curves.
  2. CUT AND FILL: UNBOUNDED — a real airport is built to its design levels; the terrain is moved; banks at the edges.
  3. EDGES: today's zone widths stand; the blend from pavement to the natural terrain happens INSIDE the graded strip / adjacent-ground zones; beyond them the DEM.
  4. ACCEPTANCE: the SIM READ is the acceptance; every law (grades, transverse, K, caps) is a DESIGN TARGET the surface aims for — the census reports, never blocks; no hard law but the pins and the physical joints.
  5. CONTACTS: FLUSH AND TANGENT — same height and no kink where bodies meet (a taxiway rolls off the runway edge; an apron continues its taxiway's slope before bending to its own surface).
  6. DATUM of an isolated body: CHAINED to the nearest pavement it touches (road or apron); only a truly detached body sits at its own terrain mean, smoothed.
  7. ROADS: smooth chains at their own caps, welded to the pavement they serve, ramping between apron levels; the ground between follows the DEM (the groundside terrace law stands).
  8. Joints only at physical separations (08k/08p) and roads between shapes per 08r-2; one pass.
* Consequence (spawner): the LP with tiers, IIS, relaxation and yield rows is REPLACED by ONE least-squares solve — a thin-plate (biharmonic) energy over the connected pavement mesh with the threshold pins as equalities, the runway chord and every law as weighted targets, the DEM fit only on the zone rings — simpler and faster, exactly the owner's ask. Spec `auto-patch-v2/design-surface-spec.md`; lane `v2smooth`. The v1 comparison instruments stay (rwy_profile, proximity diff, z−DEM by role); the bars are the sim read.

## 2026-09-09b — OWNER READ of 1.0.296 (the design surface): runways and aprons better; taxiways still undulate; adjacent ground must be LAW surfaces, never the DEM; 5 % max on all pavement (roads 8 %); floating floor/ceiling planes in the seats; THE GOAL restated

* Owner, six points: (1) runways and aprons are better; (2) taxiways still a bit excessively undulate — "they should follow terrain less and be more like runways"; (3) adjacent ground should not go directly from airside to the DEM: implement the adjacent-ground laws for drainage, clearance and runway ends and "not even try to match DEM — the engine should automatically smooth between whatever elevation we set and the DEM"; the edge zones blend smoothly with the gap interior rings; (4) all pavement including groundside has a MAX grade of 5 %; roads are the only exception at 8 %; (5) object seating: flat floor or ceiling planes float, separated from their objects — extensive at HECA, mostly around bridges at OTHH; (6) THE GOAL: "less precise faithfulness to often unreliable DEM, and more creating an environment that appears plausible to a pilot taxiing around in the simulator — as real as possible in the way roads, airside, buildings, everything is graded and placed."
* RULED (owner's law; spawner's mechanisms):
  (2) TAXIWAYS are designed like runways: each taxi chain carries a longitudinal design profile — a strong curvature (K-like) target along the centreline and the strip tie no longer drags it (see 3); `bend_taxi` senior to any coupling with ground; the 1.5 % letter cap stays a target under the 5 % ceiling.
  (3) ADJACENT GROUND is a LAW SURFACE off the pavement edge, with NO DEM term anywhere in the patch: zone 1 (the lip) drains DOWN at `lip_min_down..lip_max_down` over `lip_width_m`; zone 2 (the graded strip) continues at the strip transverse law to its outer ring; runway END corridors per the end-skirt law; the outer ring's elevation is whatever those laws give — the MESH ENGINE blends from the patch boundary to the DEM outside it (Ortho4XP's own drape). `gap_interior_ring` cells (the ground between pavements inside the airport) join the bending sheet, continuous with the zones. The `[design] dem_zone` term is DELETED; the DEM's only entries are the threshold pins and a detached body's plane datum.
  (4) A HARD CEILING of 5 % grade (`[common] pavement_max_grade = 0.05`) on every pavement class including groundside pavement, pads and lots; roads `road_max_grade = 0.08`; enforced in the active set like the runway rows; the per-class letter caps stay targets.
  (5) SEATS: a floor or ceiling plane detaching from its walls is a per-vertex cluster delta splitting one connected mesh — RULED: an object's CONNECTED geometry moves as one rigid body (one delta per connected component of the OBJ8 mesh); per-vertex deltas exist only between disconnected components; a horizontal plane is never split from the walls that carry it. Read first at one HECA site and one OTHH bridge (the owner names none — the lane finds the largest plane/wall separation in the rebake result), then fix, then the HECA/OTHH tile builds.
  (6) THE GOAL replaces the census-zero campaign goal: plausibility to a taxiing pilot; the DEM is an unreliable witness; the sim read is the acceptance (08t (4) stands). Lanes `v2ground` (2, 3, 4) and `v2seatplanes` (5), parallel.

## 2026-09-09g — OWNER: interior ground surrounded by pavement overrides the DEM (the gap interior rings keep it smooth); outer ground mostly blends into the DEM — WITHOUT erasing real cliffs, terraces, plateaus and the earthworks that carry the pavement

* Owner: "For terrain surrounded by pavement we should override DEM and our interior gap rings should keep things smooth, for outer areas we should mostly blend into the DEM, but the challenge is how to avoid erasing real cliffs, terraces, plateaus, and man made earthworks constructed to carry airport pavement."
* RULED (owner's law; spawner's mechanism — the civil-engineering DAYLIGHT LINE): (1) INTERIOR ground (every `gap_interior_ring` cell and zone cell enclosed by pavement) is part of the design sheet with no DEM term (09b (3) as built in 09e) — the DEM is overridden there. (2) OUTER ground: the bank foot is the DAYLIGHT (catch) POINT — walking outward from the boundary ring along its normal, the FIRST station where the 1:3 design slope line (`bank_slope`, down for fill, up for cut) meets the existing DEM within `bank_daylight_tol_m` (0.3 m), never nearer than `bank_min_width_m` 5 m and never farther than `bank_max_width_m` 200 m (a foot that never daylights is placed at the maximum and reported by name). Consequences by construction: a REAL EMBANKMENT under the pavement edge (the DEM falls away steeply right at the ring) daylights within the first few metres and the DEM's own slope carries the drop beyond — the earthwork survives; a PLATEAU EDGE or CLIFF beyond the ring is outside the daylight point and untouched; a real TERRACE that the DEM shows between the ring and the fixed 1:3 foot is met by the slope line where it stands and kept. Where the DEM within the first `bank_min_width_m` already slopes at or steeper than the bank slope in the fill/cut direction (an authored earthwork), the foot is at the minimum and the ground is the DEM's own bank. (3) The bank face between ring and foot is authored with intermediate rings (09f-1) so the mesh has nothing to interpolate. (4) The toe is smoothed in plan along the ring (09f) but never across a daylight discontinuity (the toe may jump where the ground does). Lane `v2daylight` after `v2bank2` lands (same file `emit/bank.py`); twins: a ring on a real embankment daylights at 5 m; a ring 6 m above flat ground daylights at 18 m; a plateau edge 40 m out is untouched; a cliff at 12 m is met at the cliff top.

V1 takes the closest threshold to the crossing, solves that runway, then sets the crossing node as an anchor for the other runway(s) to grade to, same logic as a tile seam boundary or the CIFP threshold." RULED: the runway whose THRESHOLD is nearest the crossing solves the crossing node under its own laws; that node's z becomes a PIN for every other runway through the crossing (as a seam/threshold pin), and every runway stays inside its grade and curvature laws. At CYXY 02/20's thresholds are nearest both crossings (15 m and 166 m) → 02/20 governs both nodes; 14R/32L and 14L/32R grade to them (the 1.07 m "dip" is then the law, spread as a vertical curve by the exact projection). `crossing_primary` / `runway_crossing_release` are replaced by the nearest-threshold pin. Lane `v2shore` after `v2bankblend` round 2 (same `emit/bank.py`).
* (4) PLANES FOLLOW THEIR WALLS, HELD OR NOT: a plane's carrier is the component its geometry TOUCHES (shared vertices / footprint contact within the identity spacing), nearest-distance only as the fallback; if the carrier is held the plane is held with it, if the carrier moves the plane moves — never a panel at a different height from the walls that carry it (HECA's Private Hall block, 143 after 09x). ## 2026-09-06h — Owner: "the runway should not need to sag that much" — attributed (law 3.3 m, v2 11.0 m); three rulings

* CORRECTION to 05ag/06e: the "max slack 0.015 m" quoted for HECA was the GRADE-EXCESS statistic (`solve/relax.py:579`); the metre statistic reads relief Σ 3,758 m, max 4.87 m, factor 2.5 reached (1.0.288) and Σ 3,804 m / 4.88 m (v2heca3). The relaxation is not cliff-free by that measure; the 2.5× factor bounds the grade, not the metres on long chords.
* Scout (Fable, read-only; `scout_heca_bow/`, one `why` run on main ab6d3c2a): the 05C/23C hold at s≈2218 (z 107.43, −7.8 m under the CIFP line) is hard-chained 39 hops to the 23R pin, but the LAW's ceiling there is 114.5 (−0.7) via the 1202 route G→J3→U→T4 (3,431 m, 51.46 m); the low point itself (s=2655, −11.0 m) binds on K rows only. Decomposition: (a) **stub-mouth hop shortcut 4.4 m** — `constraints/routes.py::_runway_crossings` joins a crossing to the ridge only where a clipped part CROSSES it (`ta*tb ≤ 0`); T4's 1202 centreline enters the slab diagonally and ENDS at n257, 2.4 m off the ridge (> weld 1.0 m), interior network nodes are never terminals, so taxi102's route stops at the runway ring and a 32 m perpendicular hop (0.48 m) replaces ~300 m of centreline (4.4 m); (b) **apron pav132 short-circuit of taxiway G, 2.7 m** — the binding chain runs through pav132's RELAXED apron chords (1.17–1.52 %) and junction planes instead of along G (2,819 m / 42.28 m): stage 1 of the last resort allocates slack for feasibility blind to the runway's DEM fit, so the runway sinks rather than the apron taking slack; (c) **K overshoot ≈ 3.3 m** — the DEM-fit weight (runway 20) humps the profile toward the DEM between the s≈1250 and s≈2218 holds, it arrives at −1.4 %, and K needs ≈ 680 m to turn, so the minimum lands 430 m past the hold; a straight −0.24 % run between the holds would overshoot 0.1 m.
* RULED (spawner): (a) a 1202 centreline node INSIDE the runway slab joins the ridge at the nearest station by a lateral hop over its perpendicular distance at the runway transverse cap, and interior network nodes are route terminals — a crossing need not cross the ridge to reach it; (b) the relaxation program's objective carries the RUNWAY family's DEM-fit term (the same weight the preference ladder gives it) beside the slack variance, so slack is placed where it costs the runway nothing before the runway is sunk — the runway never sags beyond what the hard chain requires; (c) the runway profile carries a SMOOTHNESS preference (a soft second-difference penalty along the ridge, weight above the runway's DEM fit, keyed in `rulesets.toml [common] runway_profile_smoothness`) so the profile runs straight between holds instead of humping to the DEM and diving. Lane `v2bow` (files: routes.py, relax.py/variance.py, runway_profile.py, pipeline weights — no overlap with v2seatclusters / v2lemd4). Bars at HECA: bow ≤ 5 m (law 3.3 + K), the s=2218 ceiling ≥ 112 (law 114.5 − hop tolerance), all six halves ≤ 1.53 %, K 0.33 pp/100 m. Also owed: 05R/23L bows −9.7 (v1 −4.4) and 05L/23R crests +11.0 (v1 +2.9) — same question, next.

The design surface moves the ground metres, so v2 has to do what v1 never needed to.

Recommended: terrain to the object for buildings with a ground footprint (a pad is already a 09c shape), objects to the terrain only for what has no footprint (small props, canopies).

## 2026-09-09ah — v2bankblend round 6 NOT MERGED (b04bcb69): 09ag's premise refuted — d_in was already the true distance; transect 1's residual is a SLIVER-TRIANGLE interpolant across a corner crease, bounded √2·Δ/D; PARKED for the HECA sim read

## 2026-09-10f — Owner: "the SPJC tile build failed, it makes no sense to fail due to a cache: 1, the cache should already be warm, and 2, if it's not why wouldn't the app just refresh it?!" — the production host now WARMS a cold frame; the refusal stays the harness's law

* The app's SPJC build (1.0.302) died in v2's loader: `REFUSING: the production DEM frame for S12W078 is COLD — NO cached airports OSM layer -12-078_airports.osm.bz2; NO airport elevation insets dir S12W078_airport_insets`. SPJC spans S13W078 (warm) and S12W078 (its north half; the app never built tile −12-078, so its layer and insets were never fetched — RULINGS 08h's owed item). The refusal is the HARNESS's frame law (`dem_production.frame_state` → `_degrade` → `ColdDemFrame`; a lane must not measure in a frame it warmed on its own schedule) and it leaked into production: the app's driver (`engine_v2.py`, `core_hosted=True`) never passes `allow_degraded`, so a two-tile airport could not build at all.
* FIX (spawner, mechanism not intent): `ProductionDem._compose` — when the frame is cold, the host is production (`core_hosted=True`), no degraded flag was accepted and the base raster is present, `_warm_tile` fetches the tile's airports OSM layer (`OSM_queries_to_OSM_layer` writes the cache when absent) and bakes every airport inset on that tile (`INSETS.ensure_insets_for_tile`, G4-safe), logs `[dem] production frame S12W078 is COLD (…) — warming it as a build of tile -12-078 would`, then re-reads the frame; a still-cold frame degrades/refuses as before. The harness path (`core_hosted=False`, the pipeline CLI) is unchanged: it still refuses and names `--refresh-data`. Twins ×3 (`test_v2coldframe_warms_in_production.py`). The v1 engine never had this check (it built SPJC on base + inset).
* Not yet exercised on the network: the owner's SPJC rebuild on app 1.0.303 is the closing test (the first build fetches; provenance carries `warmed:S12W078`).

The lane's own bar was one transect; a transect cannot see a fold beside it.

## 2026-09-10i — LEMD "components coming apart" ATTRIBUTED: the seat CUTS contact edges inside one OBJ8 placement (929 cut, 200 of 288 written members carry several deltas) and seats kilometre-wide clusters on ONE measured foot — lane `v2rigid`: one touching body, one delta, founded on feet across it

* Mechanism (v), dominant: `emit/clusters.py:299-303` cuts the contact edge between two touching GROUND components whenever their feet-founded targets differ by more than `cluster_seat_tolerance_m` 0.5 — both sides are then SEATED in different clusters and `rigid.complete_component_deltas` (free components only, `rigid.py:118`, `engine_v2.py:515`) never reconciles them. T4 car park `Terminal4_yellow-LEMD13.obj`: wall c64 +20.898 vs wall c142 +19.668 (940 pairs within 2 m), deck plates alternating between them; old terminal `OldTerminal_FSX-ZNTWR/LEMD54/tej2`: walls +5.5 vs plates +8.3 (and +8.5 vs +13.0). Inventory: 12,057 pairs of one OBJ8 within 2 m with different deltas, 85 sites > 2 m.
* Mechanism (v′): ONE-FOOT MEGA-CLUSTERS — 76 of 173 seated clusters wider than 200 m were decided by ONE measured foot: T4S k1 (4,202 parts, 29 resources, ⌀982 m, n_measured 1) at −6.507 against neighbours +2.82…+2.88 → 9.3 m tears; k2703 (⌀1,026 m, one foot) +32.492 vs its touching part +20.571. A single foot is not a measurement of a kilometre.
* (i) carrier by contact count picked the wrong wall for LEMD13's decks (c142's 20 contacts beat c64's 8; the plate sits 1.23 m below a wall it also touches); (iv) authored gaps (no carrier within 0.5 m) fell to "nearest" 1.7–3.7 m away, consecutive decks of one stack alternating deltas. (ii)/(iii) not observed. v1 cut clusters the same way (`object_clusters.py:184`) but its DEM-following ground kept deltas at +0.42 m, so cuts never showed; under the design surface deltas differ by metres and every cut is a visible tear.
* RULED (spawner, under 09z (4) "planes always follow the walls they touch" and 09af-1): (1) a contact edge INSIDE one authored placement is NEVER cut — one touching body, ONE delta; a placement has several deltas only across a physical separation (components that do not touch within `identity.min_distinct_spacing_m`); (2) a body's delta is founded on feet ACROSS it — every ground-contact component contributes its foot, the body takes the median (the residual per foot is REPORTED); a body wider than `[rebake] body_feet_span_m` with fewer than one foot per that span is not seated on what it has: it samples the design surface under every ground-contact component's footprint; (3) plates take the delta of the body they touch (contact resolved transitively from ground components), never a contact-count vote against a touching wall; (4) OTHH's deliberately separate datums (the interchange basin plates, tunnel/bridge decks, `plate_units`) are their own bodies by construction — they do not touch the walls they are exempt from; the lane proves it (deltas within 0.05 m of 1.0.300's). Bars: LEMD pairs > 2 m within one placement 85 sites → 0; the T4 car park, old terminal and T4S sites read one delta per touching body; `> 3 m` seat census ≤ 14; OTHH's 3 families unchanged.

The bending sheet's affine null space along pav28 is unpinned: its LEVEL is inherited from its far contact (686.5 m at the apron end) and extrapolated linearly east while the real DEM rises 694 → 696.4 — the cut deepens monotonically, exactly as read. Not the terrain edge (0 edge rows at CYXY), not a ramp, not a road.

* OWNER QUESTION 10o-1: (A) taxi bodies get the per-body mean datum like apron bodies (recommended: the body keeps its designed shape; only its level is told where the ground is; runway contacts stay hard), or (B) leave taxi levels to their pavement contacts (today).

## 2026-09-10p — OWNER RULES 10o-1 = (A): TAXI BODIES GET THE PER-BODY MEAN DATUM — lane `v2taxidatum`

* OWNER (interview): "Yes, taxi bodies get the mean datum." Every TAXI body (a connected group of taxi-family faces, as `apron_roles` bodies are formed today) carries ONE weak row: its mean z is the mean production DEM under its own vertices (weight `body_datum`, the same row apron bodies carry, 09p (3)); never per vertex; the runway family stays excluded (its chord and pins are its datum). The body keeps its designed shape (bend_taxi, the taxi profile); only its LEVEL is told where the ground is; runway contacts stay hard and the final projection absorbs any conflict into non-runway vertices.

## 2026-09-10q — Owner SPJC read (first v2 build, app 1.0.306): taxiway dip at −12.0284851, −77.1128014 far below the apron (−12.0292083, −77.1143534) and the parallel runway; "the runway should not be 9 m above this taxiway"; "airports want to minimize the elevation variance between adjacent paved areas" — scout

* Owner verbatim: "excessive dip in taxiway … significantly below both the apron … and the parallel runway which appears to make the apron drop steeply away from the terminal when it should be at or below 1%, and the runway also seems like it should be allowed to have a bit more curvature, as in reality airports want to minimize the elevation variance between adjacent paved areas when possible. The runway should not be 9m above this taxiway."
* Two questions in it: (a) the taxiway dip — likely the 10o class (a taxi body with no level; lane v2taxidatum in flight) or the apron's body datum; (b) the RUNWAY PROFILE LAW — today the runway targets the threshold chord (weight `chord`) with vertical curves under the hard K/max-grade laws; the owner's principle: a runway may curve within its hard laws to sit nearer the pavement beside it. Scout (Opus) measures the transect terminal → apron → taxiway → runway, `why.py` at the dip and the apron edge, and the LAWFUL ENVELOPE of the runway profile (the lowest profile through the fixed thresholds under the K and grade laws) abeam the taxiway. The runway principle comes back as an owner question with the envelope numbers before any change (08v: the runway family's laws are hard; the chord is a target).

## 2026-09-10r — OWNER PRINCIPLE (SPJC follow-up): "Ideally the apron profile would take into consideration the whole airport layout, long gentle curves are best for fast moving aircraft."

* Read with 10q ("the runway should be allowed a bit more curvature … minimize the elevation variance between adjacent paved areas"): the RUNWAY's longitudinal profile (the fast-moving aircraft) and the pavement around it are designed TOGETHER — the runway is not a straight threshold chord that everything else bends to; it is a long, gentle curve (the hard K and grade laws bound it) that considers the whole airport layout: the levels of the aprons and taxiways beside it and the ground they sit on. Today `constraints/runway_chord.py` targets the straight chord between the thresholds at weight `[design] chord`, so adjacent bodies bend to the runway (SPJC: the runway 9 m above the taxiway beside it). The direction of the change (pending the scout's envelope numbers): the chord becomes the runway's WEAK fallback and its profile is the minimum-curvature curve through the threshold pins that also minimises the level variance against the bodies it touches (their per-body datums now follow the ground mean, 10p), under the hard family laws. Spec amendment and lane after 10q's scout reports; ruled as a design principle, not yet a mechanism.

## 2026-09-10t — SPJC ATTRIBUTED: the 9.30 m = 6.7 m taxiway (a taxi body with the right MEAN but no TILT — the affine null space, not just the level) + 1.1 m runway (a straight chord where a lawful curve could sit lower) + 1.5 m real relief; the apron's 5.46 % over 85 m is unpriced past `apron_body_chord_max_m`

* Points: taxi `primary_parallel:pav49#22` node −1551 design 17.41 / DEM 24.06 (−6.65); apron `apron:pav49#35` 22.90 (+0.08); 16L/34R ridge abeam 26.71 (+2.10) — 26.71 − 17.41 = 9.30 m. BUT 10p's mean row does not close it: over pav49's 2,010 taxi vertices mean z − mean DEM = −0.39 m; the longitudinal profile runs +0.99 → −6.65 → −4.71 m against the DEM — a straight ramp under a rising ground; the error is a missing TILT (first moment), second-order, not the level. The apron's 5.46 % is unpriced: nodes −2440/−2477 are not ring-adjacent and 85 m apart, past `apron_body_chord_max_m` 60 (`emit.toml:46`), so no apron grade row sees the path.
* Runway 16L/34R: the built ridge reproduces the straight chord to ≤ 0.01 m at every 250 m station — zero vertical curves (by design: `runway_chord.py`, `chord = 300`). Lawful envelope (LP at 10 m: grade ≤ 1.5 %, end-zone 0.8 % over the outer quarters, K ≥ 300 m per 1 %): abeam the taxiway 25.60 (1.11 m lower; the binder is the 0.8 % end-zone cap); over the length a lawful curved profile cuts mean |z − DEM| 2.14 → 0.37 m.
* RULED (spawner, mechanism under 10p and 10r): (1) the per-body datum is the DEM's AFFINE FIT per body — three weak rows (mean and the two first moments: the body's level AND tilt follow the plane fitted to the production DEM under its vertices), for apron and taxi bodies alike; weight `body_datum`; the runway family excluded; the body's shape within the plane stays designed — round 2 of `v2taxidatum` (after round 1 reports; a message cannot reach a running lane this session). ## 2026-09-10af — OWNER (verbatim): "it would be a relatively small, approximately road width extension to render the door or corridor walls below the surface; whereas if the entire building is uniform across its bottom, then it's just foundations and we can take advantage of that to allow the building to sit on a sloping surface without having any portion of the building floating and no ramps needed. The author often uses this to place buildings on a slope: on the high side the full 'below ground' portion of the building is under the terrain, while the low end may have no building below ground." — FOUNDATIONS vs CORRIDORS is a property of the OBJECT'S BELOW-ZERO GEOMETRY

* The discriminator: a corridor or door well is a NARROW below-zero feature — a road-width cut between two walls a road-width apart, a small fraction of the building's footprint; a FOUNDATION SKIRT is uniform across the whole footprint (every perimeter wall shares the same below-zero extent), and its "wall pair" is the building's two long sides a building-width apart. A foundation skirt is not a corridor: the building is seated on the sloping ground with its high side buried and its low side exposed — no ramp, no trench, nothing floating (the seat already does this, 09af-1).
* RULED (spawner): Law C admission gains (d) THE NARROW-CUT TEST: (i) the wall pair's spacing (the corridor's width) ≤ `[structures] corridor_max_width_m` (road scale — measured from OTHH's 43: their spacing, then set with margin; no number in code); (ii) the placement's below-zero geometry is NOT a skirt: the below-zero perimeter length is < `corridor_skirt_fraction` of the footprint's perimeter (propose 0.5) — when the whole bottom is below zero it is foundations and the object is left to its seat. Lane `v2corridor` round 6 after round 5 (the seated-depth fix) reports: measure both quantities on OTHH's 43 and LEMD's candidates first (the round-4 discipline), implement, bars LEMD 17 → 0 / OTHH 43 identical; if OTHH's kerb pairs are wide (a two-lane kerb road), (ii) alone may be the clause — the table decides.

## 2026-09-10ag — OWNER (verbatim): "This should hopefully simplify pads and terrain flattening by improving building seating when possible. Not all buildings have this affordance and those would still require a flat pad." — A SKIRTED BUILDING NEEDS NO PAD

* Rule: a building whose object carries a FOUNDATION SKIRT (10af: a uniform below-zero extent of depth s across its footprint) is seated on the sloping ground WITHOUT a flat pad — its zero is set so no part floats (the seat at the LOW side's ground; the high side buries up to the relief) and the terrain under it keeps its design slope, as long as the relief across the footprint ≤ s (the skirt hides the intersection). A building without a skirt (no below-zero geometry) still gets the flat pad of 09c (a corner would float otherwise). Where a skirted building's footprint relief exceeds s, the excess is handled as today (a pad plane, or a cut, so the low corner never floats).
* Consequences (spawner): (1) `planar/structures` mints an `object_pad` / `building` pad only for skirt-less objects (or skirted ones whose relief exceeds the skirt); skirted objects leave the ground surface alone — fewer pads, fewer terraces, less flattening; (2) the rebake seats a skirted body at its LOW-side foot (never the median: the median would float the low side by half the relief); (3) OTHH's pits/tunnel families and 10l's pad-fronts-apron rule unchanged for the pads that remain. * RULED: adjacent-ground vertices (zone 1, zone 2, end corridors, skirts — the `graded_strip` family) carry a WEAK per-vertex DEM DATUM (weight `[design] ground_datum`, tuned); their law rows stay one-sided and ONE-WAY (ground follows pavement, never pulls it — 09b (2)/(3)'s intent stands and pavement keeps no DEM pull, 08t (1)); the strip therefore equals the natural ground wherever the ground satisfies the zone law relative to its pavement, and is cut or filled only where a law row binds; the bank (09g) then starts from a ring already on the DEM and vanishes there (foot at the minimum width, zero drop); a ring is still EMITTED (the mesh needs its vertices) but carries the DEM's own values where no law binds — so "emitted only where the DEM creates a violation" holds in value, not in geometry. Lane `v2grounddem` (Opus): spec §23 with the consumer table (zone rows, bank daylight, terrain edge, the census's strip families, `why`), twins, closing tests CYXY (the site: strip within 0.10 m of the DEM at 60.7005131, −135.0573873; the bank face there ≤ the bank slope; DEFECTs 0; crossings unchanged) and HECA (strip undulation and taxiway undulation ≤ control — the strip must not drag the taxiway edge through bending; bows unchanged; DEFECTs 0), OTHH byte-identical or every changed vertex quoted.

The only edits we would need to do to objects would be splitting large groups and ensuring each object's anchor was in the right location." — RULED (interview): THE OBJECT STAGE SWITCHES: no vertex rewrite, no seat solve; MSL → AGL in the DSF; rigid bodies split into their own objects with anchors where the terrain equals their intended zero; X-Plane drapes each on the design surface

Spec `docs/specs/auto-patch-v2/object-placement-spec.md` (Fable) then lane `v2agl`: (1) DSF text round-trip with backup + provenance (DSFTool), MSL → AGL; (2) the OBJ8 SPLIT WRITER: one file per body, vertices rigidly transformed to the new anchor (translation in the authored frame; heading preserved), header / attributes / LOD / ANIM blocks copied — a body that cannot be cut cleanly out of an ANIM or LOD block stays whole and is reported; (3) the ANCHOR RULE per body class: skirted building → its low-side foot; skirt-less building → a point on its pad; basin → a rim point; line object → per segment at the segment's mid-foot; elevated deck → merged into the building's object it abuts (11a's grouping, now "same object"), or its kerb point; (4) the DSF placement rewrite: the original placement replaced by N `OBJECT` lines (AGL) at the new anchors; (5) acceptance = the owner's LEMD and OTHH reads (feet census by anchor-terrain sampling as the report figure).

## 2026-09-11i — OWNER 11g-1 ANSWERED: a canopy on columns STAYS CONNECTED to its columns, and to an adjacent building (a roadway canopy along a terminal) — the group is ONE object; a LONG span joining two far buildings (the HECA railway class) MAY disconnect so the buildings seat; where feasible, ADAPT THE TERRAIN to the canopy-and-building group rather than split it

If it's a long thing like the railway connecting two far buildings at HECA we accept disconnecting it so the buildings can seat. But if it's feasible, adapting the terrain to accommodate the canopy and building group would be preferred."

Consequences (Fable):
* The v2bridgegroup r2 grouping (11g) stands: LEMD38/84/60 welded to canopies LEMD03/54/51 are ONE object each. Their `> 3 m` feet are not a grouping defect but a TERRAIN one — the design surface must accommodate the group.
* GROUP PAD: a welded canopy-and-building group takes ONE pad across its footprint union, the level by the pad law (10l apron-edge, 10ag skirt, pads by proximity) applied to the group; the surface ADAPTS to it where lawful. Feasibility = the adapted surface stays within the pavement laws it touches; infeasible → the long-span exception.
* LONG SPAN: a connecting body may disconnect only when it is long (HECA railway class) AND the group pad is infeasible; a law-table length, per airport where needed.
* Spec `object-placement-spec.md` §11 (Fable); lane `v2canopy`.

## 2026-09-11m — scout `v2dsfframe`: app 1.0.313's DSF writes are INTACT (LEMD 3,021 → 3,934 placements = 3,021 − 179 splits + 1,092 bodies, all three hashes match provenance; OTHH 14,230 → 15,090, 208 of 214 AGL rows converted); the `.dsf.anchor_bak` IS the pristine DSF (moved aside at write, pack-install mtime); LEMD's "0 converted" is correct (every row already plain `OBJECT`); the DEFECT is the READ FRAME — the pack read for the plan (`dsf_reader.ensure_dsf_text_path`, path-tagged key when `cache_dir=None`, untagged `<basename>.text` when the engine passes its cache) re-derives from the LIVE, post-write DSF, so a SECOND build over a written pack plans 3,934 placements (ids ≥ 3021) and the write half, dumping the backup, REFUSES — lane `v2idempotent`, app 1.0.315 before any LEMD/OTHH rebuild

* THE LAW (Fable): a pack read for the object stage ALWAYS resolves the PRISTINE DSF — `<dsf>.anchor_bak` when present, else the live file — through ONE resolver; the dump cache is keyed by the pristine file's CONTENT hash (requantisation-aware identity already exists in `dsf_write`), never by path alone, so the four contaminated cache files become unreachable without a shared-repo deletion. The restore step (§10 (1)) also REMOVES the previous write's body files named in `o4_placement_provenance.json` before writing, so a rebuild never sees `__b` files as authored objects and never writes `__b__b`. Twin: two consecutive writes over one pack copy are byte-identical (DSF sha, file list, counts).

## 2026-09-11r — OWNER READ OF LEMD (1.0.313/1.0.315 tiles): "objects are badly broken, I think maybe individual pieces, like roofs, road decks, are getting set to sit on the ground instead of staying grouped... that's a possible issue with modifying everything in the DSF since many structures are constructed from multiple objects that have to stay at their same relative height to each other" — THE SWITCH'S FIRST ADJUDICATION: the AGL placement of FOOTLESS pieces is refuted; relative heights within a structure are LAW

## 2026-09-11s — ATTRIBUTED (from the app's own `o4_v2_placement_LEMD.json`): 218 of LEMD's 1,092 split bodies were written as their own file with `y_zero` > 2 m — a roof at 69.55 m, tower parts at 65–68 m, canopies at 40 m — and the anchor rule shifted each so its LOWEST VERTEX lands on the terrain; the foot census read them as perfect. LAW (spec §13, Fable): an ELEVATED body (`y_zero` above the object's zero by more than `[rebake] elevated_base_m`) NEVER has a file of its own — it joins its CARRIER (the same placement's ground body with the largest plan overlap, else the nearest) at its authored offset; a placement with no ground body is kept whole; the census counts `elevated bodies as own files` (bar 0). Lane `v2elevated` → the next app build is the owner's re-read.

## 2026-09-11t — OWNER READ OF LEMD (basin): "the basin is looking better, but still a gap between the outer edge and the apron, so the apron elevation shape needs to be closer with less of a gap between the floor cutting shape. The object also provides a floor I believe, so that should be visible instead of seeing the terrain at the bottom of the basin."

Fable: two laws for lane `v2basinedge` (spec design-surface §24): (1) THE CUT HUGS THE WALL — the basin's cut shape (`structure_rim` / `basin_wall` ring) is the object's OUTER wall face at its top, not a widened or snapped-out ring; the apron's rim vertices sit ON that face at the rim level (10l/10bd: rim = apron edge) so the pavement meets the wall with no shelf and no gap — horizontal gap ≤ the mesh identity spacing, vertical step at the rim 0. (2) THE TERRAIN FLOOR SITS UNDER THE OBJECT'S FLOOR — the trench floor = the object's floor plate elevation MINUS `basin.floor_clearance_m` (new key, default 0.5 m) so the plate renders and the terrain never shows through; the floor law "rim − depth" reads the depth from the plate (already the 10bd reading) and subtracts the clearance. Both measured at LEMD's T4S basin on the app's 1.0.315 products first (`<Patches>/+40-010/LEMD.graded.json`), fixed, LEMD once.

## 2026-09-11u — OWNER READ OF LEMD (basin object + footbridge): "I think the basin object group is being seated too low as well, since the wall around its perimeter should extend above ground level, and there's a pedestrian bridge structure that crosses the road and should stay anchored to the terminal building but it is currently resting on the ground blocking the road."

Fable: two candidate mechanisms, attributed before any fix (scout `v2lemdread`): (1) the basin body's anchor — under AGL X-Plane sets the origin at the terrain under the anchor; an anchor inside its own trench (10bd's class) drapes the object to the FLOOR, and 1.0.313 classified no `basin` body at all (11k), so its anchor was the generic rule's; the law is §6's RIM anchor where the design surface equals the object's zero, and the wall's parapet above that zero must then stand above the apron; (2) the footbridge — either an elevated body written alone (§13's class) or a FOOTLESS PLACEMENT authored on the pack's datum (deck at y ≈ 0 over the road) that AGL drapes onto the road: the deck rule of §6 (merged into the building it abuts, one file, the building's anchor) was never wired (`merged_into` unset, v2canopy r1) — the fix joins it to the terminal's FILE (translated and heading-rotated into the terminal's frame), never a hard-coded elevation.

## 2026-09-11v — ATTRIBUTED (scout `v2lemdread`, the 1.0.315 LEMD plan): (A) THE BASIN — §6's RIM anchor is NOT WIRED (`anchor_rule.py:192`: "pads / rims are accepted and unused"); all 10 basin bodies anchor INSIDE the trench on the floor; the object (LEMD36/37 + SWbaume, parapet authored +2.99 above zero, floor −7.03) is split into per-body files each draped on its own, so ONE rigid object's zero plane SCATTERS over 7.0 m (593.30 … 600.29): LEMD37 b1's parapet lands 1.35 m BELOW the rim (597.64), SWbaume b2's 0.85 m below — the owner's "seated too low". (B) THE FOOTBRIDGE (T4 landside ≈ 40.4966, −3.5912; `Terminal4_green-LEMD16/17/18`, `Terminal4_yellow-LEMD16`, deck y 4.5–7.8, NO vertex below 3.9) is a FOOTLESS placement in unit:25 — 171 resources on ONE shared datum row (`OBJECT 293 −3.564788 40.492764`); its split segments were written alone and shifted onto the road (deck bottom 616.017 vs road 616.05), its kept-whole sibling drapes at the DATUM point (surface 595.81) and is buried 15.7 m. 89 of LEMD's 302 placements are footless (authored ymin > 0.5): 45 split (on the ground), 44 kept whole (buried by the datum offset) — `Terminal4SAT_pink-LEMD01` 179,066 m², y 14.5–25.3, KEPT: the T4S roof 20 m low. `deck_signature.elevated_deck` reads none of them; `merged_into` is set on 0 of 1,092 bodies.

THE LAW (Fable, spec §14):
* **A FOOTLESS BODY IS CARRIED.** Any body — a split body or a whole placement — with no ground contact (authored ymin above `elevated_base_m`) takes its CARRIER's anchor and offset: the footed body it abuts in the unit's abutment/contact graph (largest contact), else the nearest footed body of its UNIT in plan, else the unit's largest footed body. It is written as a body file at the carrier's anchor with the carrier's `authored_offset` (the same writer as a split body; no vertex rewrite beyond the translation every body file already carries; same heading — one unit, one row). Never at the datum, never on the ground. Census: `footless at datum` and `footless on ground` (bars 0).
* **A BASIN OBJECT IS ONE FILE** anchored at a RIM point where the design surface equals its zero (§6, now wired: `rims` exclude the interior of the body's own ring from every anchor search — 10bd's class); floor, walls and parapet share one zero, so the parapet stands its authored +2.99 above the apron. No body of a basin resource is ever draped alone.
* THE SPREAD CLASS in general: bodies of ONE resource that overlap in PLAN (a floor under walls, a ledge inside a wall) are ONE body whatever the contact graph says (10i widened by plan overlap); the split only ever separates bodies that are apart in plan AND whose terrain differs by more than `split_tol_m`.
* Lane `v2carrier`, after `v2elevated` merges (same files). One app build then carries §13 + §14 for the owner's re-read.

THE LAW (Fable, spec §15): (a) STANDS-OVER IS THE CARRIER — an elevated body's carrier is the footed body BENEATH it with the largest PLAN OVERLAP across the whole UNIT (every resource), then the largest contact, then the nearest in plan; plan overlap beats contact beats distance, and a same-resource body is not preferred. (b) PLAN-OVERLAP BINDING RE-CUTS — a bound group whose members' intended zeros differ by more than `split_tol_m` is re-cut along the terrain (a rigid body is never 1.4 km across five metres of surface). (c) THE RESIDUAL THE EYE READS — a low-side-foot body reports (its zero − the zero of the body beneath it or the ground under its feet), not its authored relief; census `stands-over float > 0.5 m` (bar 0 for carried bodies). (d) DUPLICATE ROWS — 19 Cargo resources had two identical rows at the datum; the split consumed one and the other still draws the whole object at the datum: identical rows (lon/lat/heading) of one resource are ONE placement for the split; census `duplicate rows surviving` bar 0. (e) THE INSTRUMENT — the shipped plan's `surface_z` is the MESH; the dry-run tool's is the graded surface; they disagree > 0.3 m for 214 of 874 bodies (worst 22.03 m, an off-sheet anchor): the tool marks an anchor on no graded face `off-sheet` and never compares it; a dry-run number for an off-sheet body is not evidence.
