(B) `building4` / 83 is ONE flat pad (66,207 m², z 602.28–602.47, no stacked faces): the "layers" are its boundary STEPS against parking/road neighbours (`pav124` +2.63, `pav137` +1.80, `route6` +1.80, `pav146` −1.01; the apron joints are 0.00 since 11af) — five levels around one plateau, because `classify/evidence._pads` unions every touching OSM footprint into one part at one level.

CLASS: 25 placements skipped as "no genuine solid" (roofs; 20 span ≥ 50 m; worst `Munoza-LEMD77` 30.07 m, `green-TEJ1` 24.57 off their own ground); 138 written files whose own geometry's ground departs > 3 m from the row's; 54 carried bodies whose carrier zero is > 1 m off the ground under them (worst `green-rada__b1` +13.18 on a fence at "0 m²"); pad terracing 10 pads > 0.5 m.

THE LAW (Fable, spec §16): (1) THE THICKNESS GATE IS SEAT-ERA — under `placement = agl` EVERY object row of the pack is in the plan population; a resource with no solid component is a FOOTLESS body and is carried (§14/§15), never left on the datum; the census counts `rows on the datum outside the plan` (bar 0). (2) EVERY BODY IS RE-CUT BY TERRAIN — carried, footless or skirted alike: a body whose own geometry's ground spans more than `split_tol_m` is cut into terrain groups (§9's rule over its own feet or, footless, over the ground under its footprint), each group carried by what IT stands over / anchored on its own low side; the census reads the ground under the carried GEOMETRY, never the carrier's box. (3) A CARRIER IS A SOLID — line segments, grass, signs and any body whose footprint fill (parts hull area / plan box area) is under `carrier_fill_min` (0.2) never carry; "stands over" is measured on the parts hull, not the axis-aligned box; and a carrier whose zero is more than `split_tol_m` off the ground under the carried body is refused — the search continues, and with no carrier the footless body anchors at the ground under its OWN footprint centroid (its authored y kept). Lane `v2skipped`.
* (B) is an OWNER INTENT question 11ai-1: a 66,000 m² union of touching OSM buildings is one flat plateau; its groundside neighbours (car parks, service road) terrace 1–2.6 m against it, lawful under the groundside terrace law but read as "layers". Options: (a) extend 10l to parking/service-road frontages (the SENIOR frontage sets the level; the others blend as banks); (b) split a unioned pad by terrain into several levels at the OSM footprint seams (buildings at different ground levels get their own pads); (c) leave. Recommendation (b)+(a).

* CONFLICT SURFACED (Fable's own two sentences): §16 (2)/(3) re-cut and judge a CARRIED body by the GROUND under its geometry; §15 (3) judges it by the ZERO of the body beneath. After the re-cut, a roof cut by ground over walls anchored at their low-side foot reads metres off those walls: `stands-over float > 0.5 m` carried 1 → 58; the garage pavilions `green-TEJ1`, admitted and cut into 9 ground groups, read −1.27 … +3.70 against the slab (bar 0.3 MISSED). RULED (§16a): A CARRIED BODY LIVES IN ITS CARRIER'S FRAME — it is cut WHERE ITS CARRIER IS CUT (one piece per carrier terrain group, each riding that group's zero), never by the ground under itself; §16 (3)'s ground check is applied to the CARRIER's own anchoring (a candidate whose zero is off the ground under its OWN feet by > `split_tol_m` is refused), not to the ground under the carried body; §15 (3)'s `zero − zero_beneath` is THE bar for carried bodies (0.3), and `zero − ground_under_geometry` is reported only. Round 2 (lane `v2skipped2`).
* Build time: plan stage LEMD 2.7 → 9.9 s, OTHH 28.6 → 46.4 s — surface sampling for the ground re-cut (913k reads); §16a removes the carried-body ground sampling; the remainder wants a vectorised sampler (owed, quoted). Multi-anchor resources (72 LEMD / 61 OTHH) stay skipped, reported.

## 2026-09-12d — ATTRIBUTED (scout `v2lemd320`) and RULED (Fable, spec §16c): the pack is the 1.0.320 write. ONE mechanism at sites 1–3: §16b (1)'s cut has the TRIANGLE as its atom (`obj8_split.BodyCut`; `tri_owner` senior; unowned triangles to the nearest body), so terrain-group boundaries run THROUGH connected solids — `OldTerminal_FSX-HANG3` (7 components → 10 files, 14 torn seams in the vault, worst 3.05 m); `Terminal4_green-LEMD50` (2 components, 86 triangles → 7 files spread 11 m, four of the carriers taxi-sign panels); `Bridge2` (3 → 8, seam 11.7 m); `Terminal4_green-STRT4` (58 components → 53 files; the deck slabs c0/c2/c3/c41/c42 each across 39 bodies; `__b44` a 1 km slab on `PKT4__b0` at 611.5 with its pier feet at 626 — §16b (3) tested the piece's MEDIAN ground, the underpass floor; worst seam 16.29 m). Written-frame class: 2,554 torn seams, 1,994 > 0.3 m, 63.9 % of bodies; 19 one-component resources in ≥ 2 files. Site 4 (T2) is a SECOND mechanism: §15 (1)(a)'s largest-plan-overlap carrier picks `LEMD38`'s 129,113 m² floor pieces (re-cut to three zeros 0.91 m apart) over the 158 m² walls the roofs rest on → roofs 0.57–0.70 m below wall tops; `LEMD58`/`LEMD50`/`T2CSG` skylight strips torn into two files 0.30 m apart; the one roof bound to a wall (`LEMD41__b1` on `LEMD52__b0`) reads 0.01 m.
* LAW §16c: the connected component is the atom of every cut (line segments and basin arcs the only station cuts); the carrier question once per component, the fallback bounded on the component's CONTACT ground not its median; a foot over a structure cut is not a ground foot (to be MEASURED at `PKT4__b0` before implementing); the carrier is what the body RESTS ON (nearest top beneath the base plane; overlap breaks ties); the torn-seam census on the written files is the bar instrument. Lane `v2atom` (Opus). Counterfactual files 3,561 → ≈ 899.
* The census the lanes quoted ("carried float 0") could not see any of this: the seam between two files of one component is invisible to every per-body number. The written-frame seam census is the instrument from now on.

## 2026-09-12f — OWNER (12c-1 answered): "No, a road sharing an edge with an apron stays apron, the connection to groundside must be a free road joining the groundside shape at a mouth"

* RULED into §27 (Fable): the free-road ruling stands — a `service_road` face sharing an edge with airside pavement IS apron (LEMD's 61 flip with the 13 lots). The groundside exemption is a MOUTH: a groundside shape is groundside only when every airside contact it has is a free road meeting it END-ON at the road's mouth (the strip's end cap, its cross-section — never a lateral edge). Flips propagate along lateral shared edges (a lot beside a road that became apron is beside apron) and stop at mouths; classification iterates to the fixpoint. Lane `v2airsideedge` extended in flight.

## 2026-09-12g — MEASURED (scout `v2plantime`), the build-time regression of 12a: the placement stage is `MeshElevationSampler.sample_at`'s per-query LINEAR SCAN over 1,771,765 retained triangles (60 % of the tile — LEMD's box keeps the tile's densest region; the sampler's "airport-bounded subset, a few thousand queries" premise is false on both counts) at ~2.0 ms per call. `build_plan` on the shipped 1.0.320 frame, mesh sampler, 3 foreground runs: 302,532 `_surface` calls (165,114 unique), 606–631 s; at 73fa97af (pre-§16b): 207,045 calls, 426–470 s. * RULED (Fable, mechanism): (1) `MeshElevationSampler` gets the uniform-grid index its own comment prescribes, bit-identical answers to the linear scan (same triangle at shared edges — candidates visited in ascending triangle index); (2) the engine's `_surface` carries `.many` (vectorised through the same index) and a `(lat, lon)` memo; (3) bars: `build_plan` on the mesh sampler ≤ 15 s at LEMD on the 302,532-call frame, elevations identical on every real query point, ONE `--engine v2 --tile 40 -4` LEMD tile build (foreground, `--no-ledger`) with the mesh step back under 1.0.319's 456 s. ## 2026-09-12r — OWNER INTERVIEW (four answers, verbatim):
* 11ar-1 (rail bores): "I may be misunderstanding the question, but we should never emit anything for actual tunnels, only the tunnel mouths and entrance/exit ramps, since the tunnel itself is underground. This applies to both rail and highway. The one exception is case like at EGLL where there are shallow tunnels with object based roofs that need an open trench to set the tunnel objects in."
* 11ai-1 (pad terracing at `building4`): "Grade frontages only" — keep the one plateau; extend the frontage law so parking and service-road faces meet the pad at its level.
* §8 seat retirement: "How do the two systems compare? Seems like we end up modifying a similar number of resources, what determines which is the better system?" — answered in session (below); decision pending.
* The two shared-repo cache files: "Leave them."

RULED from the first: a TUNNEL EMITS ONLY ITS MOUTHS AND RAMPS — rail or highway; the bore between the mouths is underground and emits nothing; a tunnel whose mouths both lie outside the airport patch emits NOTHING inside it; the one exception is the object-roofed shallow tunnel (the `tunnel_objects` / `[cutout]` class, EGLL) whose trench is open to seat the tunnel objects. Scout `v2tunnelmouths` attributes what LEMD's three rail tunnels and the highway tunnels emit between and beyond their mouths today (structures records, corridor faces, rims, decks, `tunnel_wall` bands) before the law is written. From the second: design-surface spec §28 (frontages) — lane `v2frontage`.
* The seat vs placement comparison (session's answer to the owner): both touch every object of the pack, but the SEAT rewrote the authored VERTICES of each object to follow the mesh (walls bent, floors warped, plates left where the DEM was wrong — the DEM is the unreliable witness, 09b), and the deformed files were the pack's own; PLACEMENT never deforms authored geometry — it partitions objects into rigid bodies (restorable originals), places each row AGL, and lets the DESIGN SURFACE (already graded under law: pads, basins, rims) absorb the DEM error under the anchors. The authored relations between parts stay exact; the error lives in the terrain where the law can see and price it. What decides is the sim read (09b(6)): 11ac "LEMD with 316 looks great" adjudicated the switch, and the seat's residue (floating plates, 218 roofs censused green while broken, 11r) had no lawful reading. Under the no-degradation-shield law a dying mechanism is not kept as a fallback.

## 2026-09-12x — OWNER (goal statement, verbatim): "0.1m residual is probably fine. Be sure it's clearly documented, our goal is to increase the realism of a simulated airports terrain so that it looks believable to a pilot viewing the world from inside an airplane cockpit, so sharp, unnatural cuts, or rises, things that would effect the airplanes motion are critical, but centimeter accuracy or anything invisible to the pilot is not important. Interview me if you have any questions or see ways the goals should be clarified or refined."

* 12w-1 CLOSED: the 0.1 m pad residual through the two-way groundside-ramp rows is ACCEPTED; documented in spec §28's MEASURED block as an accepted residual, not a bar.
* THE GOAL, restated as law (refines 09b(6)): the acceptance is what a pilot sees and feels from the cockpit. CRITICAL: sharp or unnatural cuts and rises, and anything that would affect the airplane's motion. NOT IMPORTANT: centimetre accuracy, and anything invisible to the pilot. Every bar in every spec is read against this: a bar that prices what the pilot cannot see or feel is a report, never a gate. Interview follows (12y).

## 2026-09-12y — OWNER INTERVIEW on the goal (four answers): thresholds "Pavement 0.05 m, visual 0.5 m" — any step or ridge over 5 cm on runway, taxiway or apron is critical; a seam, float or terrace under 0.5 m off-pavement is invisible and reported only; view range "Taxi plus approach" — everything inside the airport boundary at taxi scale, plus the terrain a pilot sees on final and climb-out within a few km at coarser scale; landside "Visual only, natural shapes" — no cliffs, floating or buried buildings, torn objects; grade laws there are targets never gates, terraces under the visual threshold are fine; build budget "10 minutes per tile" — speed work is dispatched only when a tile exceeds ten minutes.
* RULED into `design-surface-spec.md` §31 THE COCKPIT FRAME (the reading rule for every bar in every spec) and `object-placement-spec.md` §17 (its object-stage reading). Lane `v2cockpit`: the harness census and the placement census print the pilot's frame — CRITICAL (motion: steps/ridges > 0.05 m on rolled-on pavement; visible: cuts, rises, seams, floats > 0.5 m within the boundary or the approach corridor), REPORT (everything else) — from the existing families, no new instrument.

## 2026-09-12ap — ATTRIBUTED (scout `v2pavefeet`, frame reproduced byte-for-byte): the > 0.5 m pavement-foot class is 240 bodies / 1,628 feet, and by MECHANISM: 66 % (1,082 feet) is a WHOLE-BODY OFFSET — an anchor COPIED from another body by §16c (7)'s contact binding (`placement_atom.py:586-611` copies the senior's entire `Anchor`, no height test; 692 bodies share an anchor; 41 of the 76 SUNK bodies are anchored > 2 m from their own feet, median 37.5 m, max 270 m — the `TABOX/TABOXzwei/TAPSL` row of 12 GSE boxes bound over 154 m of apron, each ~1.9 m into the apron), 30 % AUTHORED relief (483 feet — and 360 of the 561 FLOATING feet are vertices at their own authored height above a correctly seated body: sign panels etc. inside `contact_band_m` 1.0 — the model's, not ours), 4 % TERRAIN. The worst two (`LEMDblast__b1` +7.18, `Terminal4sBlue-STRT4__b1` −5.08) are 81 m LINE bodies straddling the T4S tunnel-trench rim; the replay's plan-Delaunay sampler interpolates ACROSS the rim and the 55 m role grid answers `apron` over the trench hole — an INSTRUMENT question (the app samples the mesh) to settle before any object rule for them. Instrument naming defects: the reason string "authored relief N m" prints the zero-plane spread (terrain), and a bound body prints its SENIOR's reason (12ao's "low-side 4,454 feet" table was built on that field).
* RULED (Fable): (A) §16c (7) binds across members only while the bound body's OWN-GROUND zero is within `visual_m` 0.5 of the senior's zero (a box a metre below its apron is a visible burial; a seam between two different resources is not a torn solid) — beyond it the body keeps its own anchor, counted; a cluster's zero-plane span is capped the same way. (B) §17 judges a body at its GROUND-CONTACT feet — the in-band feet within `split_tol_m` of its lowest — never every vertex of the 1 m band (`contact_band_m` is shared law and stays). (C) a LINE station is cut where the surface under it spans more than `split_tol_m` (relief-driven, never a uniform N; §10 (4)'s uniform limit stays refuted). (E) the replay sampler and the role lookup honour graded HOLES (a foot over a `tunnel_trench` hole reads the trench, never the apron across it); the reason strings name the body's own anchor and call terrain spread terrain. Lane `v2pavefeet` after `v2reach` merges (both touch `placement_atom.py`). The scout's per-body projection becomes `obj8_split_report --motion-rows` on its second use.

## 2026-09-13b — OWNER: "I'm building NZQN now, looks like the console reported zero coverage for that area, did it fail to get the 1m lidar?"

* ATTRIBUTED (session): it never tried. The inset cache `Elevation_data/-50+160/S46E168_airport_insets/` was cut on 2026-09-12 13:05 by the 1.0.324 engine, whose LERC path returned an empty asset list ("skipping NEWZEALAND1M" — a MISSING CAPABILITY) and the index recorded `NZQN: NEWZEALAND1M = "no-coverage"` — a DURABLE negative, indistinguishable from a provider that truly has no data. `complete.json` marks the cache complete; today's 1.0.325 build read the index, took `COPERNICUSGLO30` (provenance `NZQN:COPERNICUSGLO30`, "source pixel 30.0 m, inset coverage 100 %"), and no LERC line appears at all. The status "no-coverage" was minted by a skip, not by a probe.
* RULED: (1) a provider skipped for a missing capability records a DISTINCT status (`unavailable:<reason>`), never `no-coverage`; (2) a `no-coverage` recorded for a LERC-compressed provider by an engine that could not decode LERC (engine version < 1.50.1771, from the index's own record — the index carries the fetch date and probe stamps; the lane finds the honest discriminator) is UNVERIFIED and is re-probed on the next build, the re-probe logged as a recorded refresh with its reason (it completes a degraded cache; it is not a content refresh) — the app's own build path; harness builds keep refusing and name `--refresh-data dem`; (3) the immediate remedy for the owner's tile is an explicit `build_airport.py NZQN --refresh-data dem` (recorded in the ledger) or deleting `S46E168_airport_insets/` — an owner act. Lane `v2insettier` (Opus).

* The clue the session proposes (to be MEASURED by lane `v2frontagestep` before adopting): a HILLSIDE building is one whose own FOOTPRINT ground changes by a storey — CYXY's pad rings span 5.5 / 4.8 m of DEM (the scout's numbers); a terminal whose ring's ground is near-level but whose lot's DEM reads +3–4 m at the frontage (SPJC, a Copernicus 30 m DSM inset — canopies, parked cars and structures sampled as ground; CYXY's inset is a 1 m HRDEM terrain model) is not cut into anything. Two-condition rule: a pair is a hillside terrace (no frontage row) only when the pair's median DEM step exceeds `frontage_step_max_m` AND the pad ring's own DEM span exceeds it; otherwise the frontage row stands and the lot grades to the pad, sloping away under its cap — SPJC's five pairs. ## 2026-09-13v — v2othhdatums MERGED (a668723a, lane 5fc707eb): §16e (1)–(2) — OTHH's nine tunnel walls back on the band (crest − ground +3.98…+19.54 → −0.00…+0.21; 0 of 9 over 0.3 m), the eight drainage basins byte-identical, LEMD 1.0.325 byte-identical (2,109 body rows, 0 changed); `seat_feet_census.py --placement-plan --mesh` had NEVER run (bounds order lat/lon vs lon/lat at `plan_bounds()`; fixed with an end-to-end twin); `v2_rebake_replay.py plan` now arms the shared-repo write guard (its DSF dump lands in the mounted mod cache — the 12j class). Suite 1,186/0 twice on main. Bars MISSED, carried as owed: (a) `Bridge_01` deck top 5.52 → 3.23 against land 3.96 (0.43 short — the deck now sits BELOW the bank); (b) §16e (3) bridge clutter: ATTEMPTED TWICE, both moved the bar backwards (cross-bridge carriers 2 → 3 → 5), code deleted under the attempt cap — the lane's intent question: "what names a bridge when neither the row nor the ring does?" (OTHH unit:6 puts Bridge_02/03/06, 250 m apart, on ONE row at ONE AGL, so `family_key` calls all 30 members one family; the deck's ring does not partition the clutter — `Bridge_02_CLUTTER_000` stands inside Bridge_06's ring); (c) `Bridge_04`/`Bridge_05` deck members carry `parts 0` — no body is formed, nothing to anchor (deck tops 6.43 vs land 3.96); (d) plan stage 72 → 80 s at OTHH (≤ 60 s bar missed on both arms — pre-existing, the 13 owed item); (e) §14 footless-at-datum 3 → 3 (the graded frame's 4 was a sampler difference).

* FABLE'S RULING on (b), spec §16e (3) amended: a bridge is named by CONTACT WITH THE DECK'S OWN MODEL FOOTPRINT, not by row and not by ring. The deck member's mesh, projected to plan, is the deck's footprint polygon (the ring is its bbox and is too coarse where decks overlap in plan). A pier or clutter body BELONGS to the deck whose footprint polygon contains its plan centroid (or lies within 0.5 m of it); where two decks' footprints both contain it, the deck whose underside is nearest ABOVE the body's top (absolute vertical distance, the §16c (4) rule) wins. A body no deck footprint contains has NO bridge family and takes the ordinary §16c rest-on ground — never filtered, never carried by a deck. `family_key` stays what it is for every other class; the bridge family is a derived relation computed once per plan, reported per body (`bridge_of`). On (c): a partless deck member IS a body — admit it in `placement_body._raw_bodies` with its footprint from the model's declared bounds and its deck-top from its plate; a datum member with parts 0 is the §16 (1) population class, not a skip. On (a): the deck top is seated to the LAND datum at the abutments (the owner's 13k words: "seating the top deck to align with the ground and let the feet land where they may"); the bar is |deck top − land| ≤ 0.5 m (§31 visual) at each abutment, feet unconstrained. Lane `v2bridgecontact` dispatched with (a)–(c); (d) stays owed.

## 2026-09-13af — OWNER on KCLT item 9 (13ab's proposal), verbatim: "Item 9, proposal approved, whenever feasible, keep object families together" — RULED (Fable, spec §16f): AN OBJECT FAMILY STAYS TOGETHER. The proposal approved: the KCLT terminal complex (the `paredes_*_charlotte` / `techos_*` / `suelos_interiores_charlotte` / `Charlotte_Airport_00{5,8}_ALB` members, all refused as basins under 09ag rule 5b — "a slab sitting on datum relief, not a sunken solid" — reading 4.25–9.54 m under the LOCAL ground and ~0 under their own RENDER datum) takes ONE datum for the whole family, seated at the terminal pad's level; pieces that stand apart from the pad are cut to their own ground where they stand (§16c). The general law: a FAMILY is the pack's set of members that (a) share one authored datum plane (the same placement row set at one authored y — the §16c (6)/(7) unit is its unit of contact) AND (b) form one connected plan cluster (footprints in contact within `contact_eps_m`, or overlapping); a family's bodies take ONE zero plane (the datum of the emitted `building` pad their footprints mostly stand on, else the median ground under the family's own contacts), and are censused as a family (`family_of`, per-family zero spread, members cut apart). "Whenever feasible": a family whose members do NOT form one plan cluster (OTHH's bridge clutter beside the deck plate — 13ae) is not held together; the feasibility test is stated, measured and reported per family, never assumed. Rule 5b's refusal stays for a genuine slab-on-relief SINGLE member; a family's slab is the family's floor. Lane `v2family` dispatched (KCLT frame; LEMD and OTHH byte-identity or every change named — LEMD's shared-datum rows (2,035 of 2,109 bodies on two rows) are the trap: two rows do NOT make one family; the plan-cluster test does).

I continue to have reservations about the bank_foot class of shapes and question whether we keep it at all, but it definitely seems to have issues at SPLP as well, cutting across other shapes because the airport is cut in half and has to be made as two patches in two tiles."

* SPLP straddles the −77° meridian (tiles −13−078 west and −13−077 east); the coordinate is 4.6 m east of the seam. Session's leads for the scout: (a) each tile builds its own SPLP patch clipped at the tile edge, so the patch COVERAGE has an edge along the meridian that is not an airport edge — §37 (3)'s bank (the 1:3 transition from coverage to DEM, emitted where load-bearing) would then run along the tile seam, across the runway and aprons: the "cutting across other shapes"; (b) the two half-patches are solved independently, so their runway vertices on the seam need not agree: the "rough spots"; (c) the texture tear is a mesh/orthophoto seam question (Triangle4XP / mask side), to be separated from (a)–(b) by measurement. The owner's standing reservation on the bank class is recorded: after 13j item 8, 13l item 2 and this, the bank survives only where it is load-bearing (§37 (3)) and NEVER along a tile seam; whether it survives at all is an owner decision after the 1.0.327 read. Scout `v2splpseam` dispatched: both tiles' SPLP patches on main, the seam rows named.

## 2026-09-13ah — OWNER (verbatim): "we have clear laws (or we should) regarding seam boundaries and how they must be kept at DEM and treated as an anchor like CIFP thresholds that everything else grades to." — RULED (Fable, spec §38): THE TILE SEAM IS A PIN. The law stood since user 2026-07-04 ("treat the seam like a runway edge") and owner 2026-07-24/26 ("the tile seam at ALL points must be anchored at DEM"; "every AIRSIDE cut-back edge … DEM-anchored HARD"), but `constraints/seams.py` (M3a) implemented it as a PREFERENCE row (`Linear.soft`, "the LP honours every seam vertex exactly wherever the law graph allows and deviates by the MINIMUM only where two seam values cannot both be reached lawfully") — softened 2026-09-04 for the SPLP class (a runway edge at 55.51 m and a strip vertex at 57.00 m on the same seam line, 1.46 m over the zone band + strip chain between them), with a deviating vertex REPORTED as a "seam residual" and not published as a pin. That softening is OVERRULED: a seam vertex is a `Pin` — the same object as a CIFP threshold, its column eliminated by `solve/rows._reduce`, held EXACTLY — and everything grades TO it. Where two seam pins cannot both be reached under a hard family between them (the SPLP runway class), the FREE vertices between them yield and the hard family is re-fitted between the pins the way §21.2 already re-fits the runway chord between thresholds (spec line "takes a CIFP threshold or a tile-seam pin, and re-fits its chord"); the pin↔pin exemption (`constraints.seam_exempt`) stands (terrain against terrain); a family that cannot be met between two pins is NAMED in the design report with its pins and the demanded vs allowed metres — never a moved pin, never a silent residual. The bank (§37 (3)) is NEVER emitted along a tile seam: the coverage edge at a seam is a pin line already at the DEM, so the transition there is zero by construction, and any bank chain crossing the seam is a defect. Scout `v2splpseam` (13ag) reads SPLP against this law; the lane follows its attribution.

* FABLE'S RULING (spec §37 (6)): A GROUNDSIDE ROAD IS A RAMP FROM ITS AIRSIDE CONTACT TO THE DEM. The owner's words (13j item 5): "service roads sloping to DEM … the airport should ideally grade to raw DEM, with airside, buildings, and lots graded smooth". Neither weight wins by contest: the road's profile is DERIVED along its route the way §34 (1) prices a tunnel ramp and §36 (5) an EAT ramp — from each airside contact (the mouth where the road meets apron/pad/lot, whose level the airside sets — airside is king), the road target is `max(DEM(s), z_contact − road_cap × s)` along the route distance s, i.e. it descends at the cap until it reaches the DEM and follows the DEM from there (and climbs at the cap where the DEM rises above the airside contact); between two contacts the two ramps meet at their higher envelope. That target is a DESIGN TARGET at design-target weight (the class §31 (3) names, not a `preferred_road_z` soft fit), with a HARD ceiling `z ≤ target + visual_m` so smoothness can never lift the road back onto the fill, and the road's own longitudinal cap and transverse cross-section stand. The bank then daylights only the ramp's short fill at the contact — the fill the owner saw is gone with the road. ## 2026-09-13ap — LANE STARTUP COST (owner: "How can we dramatically improve the startup cost of each lane?" → "implement all 5"). MEASURED on today's transcripts: before its first edit or measurement a lane spent 12–16 turns and 115k–170k FRESH input tokens (v2rampwalk 68 turns / 316k; scout v2splpseam 35 / 260k) slicing four monoliths — RULINGS.md 3,760 lines (~217k tokens), design-surface-spec.md 6,966 (~123k), object-placement-spec.md 3,768 (~64k), tools/INDEX.md 203 rows (~69k) — a quarter to a third of each lane's 350k–550k. LANDED (commit above): (1) `tools/docq.py` — `spec '§37'` prints the section and every `§37.x` / `§37 (n)` sub-block with their MEASURED blocks and nothing filed between them, `spec '§37 (6)'` one sub-block, `--object` for the object spec, `ruling 13am` one entry with its bullets, `index` one line per tool (~3k tokens), `index <name>` the full row; a miss is a named refusal; the monoliths stay the source of truth (running lanes append to them on their branches) — the split is served, not copied; (2) `tools/brief_pack.py` — a self-contained `docs/briefs/<lane>.md` assembled VERBATIM through docq and the frames registry with the standing discipline appended, so the session never retypes law and the lane reads one file; (3) the short index (docq `index`); (4) RESUME, DON'T RESPAWN — a follow-up round on the same area goes back to its lane by `SendMessage` (today's roads work ran as three fresh lanes on the same files, ~400k of startup); (5) `tools/harness/frames.py` + `docs/frames.jsonl` — captures / rebake plans / products registered with base sha and lane, `[MISSING]` when the path is gone, five entries seeded. `lane.md` / `scout.md` carry the startup protocol; CLAUDE.md the rule; twin `tests/test_docq.py` (9). Expected: startup ~120–170k → ~30–40k fresh tokens and 12–16 → 3–4 turns; nothing sacrificed if the pack is complete — the pack names its base sha and the lane still merges main first.

Suite 1,499/0 twice on main. The lane's question: is the 2 % cross-section a LAW or a §31 (3) target for a landside road?

* FABLE'S RULING (§37 (8)): FOR A GROUNDSIDE ROAD THE CROSS-SECTION IS A §31 (3) VISUAL TARGET, NOT A LAW. The owner's frame (12y): landside is "visual only, natural shapes"; a road that follows a hillside carries the hillside's crossfall, and a 2 % section forced on it is the fill the owner saw. The 2 % cross-section stays LAW on airside roads (a road inside or edge-sharing an apron IS the apron — the free-road ruling) and on a groundside road within its airside contact's reach (the ramp's first `pad_frontage_m` from the mouth, where a vehicle transitions); beyond that reach it is a design target priced under the visual family (0.5 m over the road width) and the census REPORTS it. `check_grade`'s road pair selection is re-pointed onto the same `road_pair_reading` with that reach rule (the next roads lane; a `road_cross_section` row on a groundside road beyond the reach is REPORT, not DEFECT). The five new cockpit motion rows are named by the lane before the owner's read; the app build (1.0.327) proceeds — the owner's read is the acceptance.

## 2026-09-13bb — OWNER (verbatim): "The base engine should be correcting road banking, confirm that is running before we try to grade it it or test it?" — CONFIRMED RUNNING, NOT HOLDING, and 13ba's §37 (8) is WITHDRAWN. The check: `constraints/roads.py` mints a `road_cross_section (2026-08-25g)` `Diff` row for every transverse pair of every road-family face (groundside included) at `cap.transverse` 2 %, priced at `[design] law = 300` (a one-sided quadratic penalty — "a law where the geometry allows, a design target where it cannot"), NOT in `[design] hard_rulings`; the v2 verify (`verify/within`) prices exactly those rows. On KCLT the verify counted 781 violated cross-section pairs on the CONTROL (before the ramp existed) and 462 on the ramp arm — the banking correction has been LOSING on KCLT's groundside roads all along, and §37 (6)'s hard ramp ceiling (300,000) now outranks it wherever the two disagree. 13ba read that loss as a reason to demote the law; the owner's reading is right: a road on a hillside keeps its crossfall by sitting on a BENCH (cut on the uphill kerb, fill on the downhill), not by tilting with the ground — that is the natural shape. RULED (§37 (8) rewritten): THE 2 % CROSS-SECTION IS LAW ON GROUNDSIDE ROADS TOO. The ramp target (§37 (6)) is the road's CENTRELINE profile at each station; the two kerbs follow the centreline through the cross-section rows (≤ 2 % × half-width apart), so the hard ramp ceiling binds ONE value per station (the centreline's) and never a kerb against a different station; the adjacent ground beside the road takes the bench (§19 / §37 (3)'s bank where load-bearing). `road_cross_section` on a road-family face joins `[design] hard_rulings` (the head `road_cross_section (2026-08-25g)`), so the polish certifies it like the pad ceiling. Lane `v2roadramp` round 3 (resumed): FIRST attribute the 462 rows on its existing build with `--why-at` (which two kerbs, their ramp targets and stations — the suspected mechanism is the route frame handing the two kerbs of one station DIFFERENT stations or routes, so the per-vertex ceiling tilts the section), THEN fix. Bars: v2 verify `road_cross_section` 462 → ≤ 20 with every survivor named; the v1 census `road_cross_section` 642 → ≤ 330 (the base) once its selection reads the same frame; `dsf:pol51` follow ≥ 0.85 holds and its section ≤ 2 % at every station; `dsf:pol82` ≤ 0.5; cockpit motion ≤ 7 with the five new rows named or gone; LEMD/CYXY dry as before; ONE KCLT build against `ctlkclt70646dc8`. APP 1.0.327 IS HELD until this lands — the owner asked for the confirmation before the test.
